#5162. Grand Theft Crypto? GTA 6’s Fictional Coin Becomes an $18M Meme as DacityCoin Rockets 150x

GTA 6 meme coins are here: DacityCoin hit $18M as the game launched. PumpSwap volume doubled for a day, but meme coins and PUMP stay weak.

DacityCoin (DCTY) hit an $18 million market cap, the day Rockstar Games released Grand Theft Auto VI. But what is DacityCoin? Traders were racing to find GTA 6 meme coins before the game had even finished downloading.

The Solana meme is named after a fake cryptocurrency inside the game. Life imitating GTA, you could say. DCTY traded near $0.019 at 11:00 UTC, up more than 150x in 24 hours.

What the fuck? pic.twitter.com/cWJV4vGKM2

DacityCoin’s fomo doubled PumpSwap’s daily volume on Wednesday, yet the exchange was busier than it was on 12 days since October 1. Dogecoin, Pepe and Bonk barely blinked, and Pump.fun’s own token, PUMP, is sitting near a one-month low.

So is this the spark for a new run in meme coins, or just a fun one-day side quest?

EXPLORE: Bitcoin Bounces From $62K: Is Gold and Silver’s 14% Crash Sending Money Into Crypto?

DacityCoin started as a background joke. In Rockstar’s launch trailer, the name is painted on a stunt plane. Then this week, gameplay footage from Brazilian streamer Combo Infinito showed an in-game phone app listing “DacityCoin (DCTY)”, complete with buy and sell buttons.

Crypto traders did what crypto traders do: within hours, the Solana token with the same ticker went vertical.

Before the footage, DCTY had never been worth more than about $54,000. Here’s what its main pool on DEX Screener shows now:

One important detail: Rockstar hasn’t linked the in-game coin to any real token, and DCTY has no official tie to GTA 6 or Take-Two. The name game is getting messy, too.

A separate Pump.fun token called “Dacity Coin” hit a $10.8 million market value on Wednesday, then slid to about $800,000. Exchange Bitrue joined in as well, listing DacityCoin futures with up to 10x leverage on Wednesday.

If a fresh meme coin season were kicking off, Pump.fun is where you’d see it first, since that’s where most new Solana tokens of this kind are born. So we pulled PumpSwap’s daily trading from DefiLlama, which tracks exchange data across blockchains.

Outside the GTA hype, the bigger meme coins are having a rough year. All eight large meme coins we tracked on Binance are down for 2026, by a median of 54%, while Bitcoin is down 28%. The past week stung too, with drops between 11% and 23%:

Source: Binance daily closes, Coinspeaker calculation, November 19.

Zoom out, and DCTY is still tiny. The whole meme coin sector is worth $22.5 billion on CoinGecko, with Solana’s share at only $2 billion. One token can steal the spotlight for a day, but a real run for meme coins needs the big names and overall trading to rise together. So far, that hasn’t happened.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Top Solana Meme Coins to Bid

Pump.fun did get a payday out of the DacityCoin rush. The platform earned $1.62 million in revenue on Wednesday, up from a $1.28 million average over the five days before. Since April, Pump.fun says it spends half its revenue buying PUMP on the open market, so a day like Wednesday means roughly $800,000 of buying.

The worst day was November 14, when PUMP dropped 15%. Token trackers list a scheduled release of locked PUMP tokens in mid-November, though they don’t agree on the exact date. So far, buybacks of under $1 million a day haven’t been enough to soak up that selling.

GTA 6 meme coins are hot now, but the thing to watch is breadth, meaning how many coins move together. If PumpSwap volume stays above $300 million a day after launch week and Dogecoin or Pepe join in, DacityCoin could be the start of something bigger.

If DCTY fades and volume slips back to $150 million, it’ll go down as a fun launch-day story and not much more.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Reveals Upcoming Binance Listings

#5161. Cardano Founder's Midnight Secures Major Japan Payments Deal

The news comes soon after a key breakthrough for Midnight in the United Kingdom, where Monument Bank started tokenizing customer deposits on the blockchain.

Per a Nov. 19 NADA NEWS report, Midnight Foundation President Fahmi Syed said the foundation had inked an MOU (memorandum of understanding) with a major Japanese payments company.

Syed did not name the company. But the deal is a big one for Midnight’s bid to establish a presence in Japan’s financial services sector.

However, it is just an initial agreement, and no commercial services have been rolled out in Japan yet.

It is also worth noting that the Midnight Foundation is talking to some major Japanese banks and companies working with intellectual property and medical data.

According to Syed, he spent two days in Tokyo meeting with major financial institutions and other companies.

Japan has been a key market for Midnight, along with the United States.

According to Midnight Foundation Japan country manager Taro Kimura, Japan is the only Asian country where the foundation has a local representative on the ground.

Midnight has been eyeing Japan more keenly due to the country’s sizeable Cardano community.

The Japanese push comes hot on the heels of a historic development in the British banking sector.

On Nov. 18, British financial institution Monument Bank started tokenizing retail customer deposits on Midnight. It is the first UK bank to launch such a product on a public blockchain.

The first phase of the venture involves up to £250 million in tokenized deposits. For this, the bank is offering customers a 12-month fixed annual interest rate of 5.2%.

Midnight employs zero-knowledge cryptography to safeguard sensitive transaction details, unlike traditional public blockchains. As a result, banks can meet compliance requirements while keeping their customers’ private information and transaction details out of public view.

#5160. Why This Analyst Isn’t Rushing to Buy XRP Below $1 Despite Predicting an Explosive Rally to $10

XRP’s return below the psychologically important $1 level hasn’t convinced every investor that it is time to buy the dip. Despite believing the payments-based token could eventually climb to a staggering $10, one trader is holding back. His cautious stance raises an interesting question: if XRP has such massive upside potential, why is he waiting on the sidelines?

In a November 19 post on X, crypto trader VirtualBacon identified the $1 price level as a potential bottoming zone for XRP in the current market cycle. The analyst suggested that the cryptocurrency may be approaching the lower end of its correction, even as selling pressure continues to weigh on its price.

“For me, $1 is roughly the bottom area for this cycle,” he stated.

While he described the $1 mark as the approximate bottoming zone for XRP during the ongoing cycle, he did not suggest that a definitive price floor had already been established.

His hesitation stems from the belief that investors will still have plenty of opportunities to accumulate alternative cryptocurrencies even after the broader market begins recovering. In his view, many altcoins could remain relatively cheap early in the next market rebound, meaning investors don’t necessarily need to rush into XRP before its downtrend runs its course.

“I’m not buying it yet,” he explained, making it clear that he would rather wait for a more favorable entry point than rush into the market.

XRP was trading at $0.9647 at the time of publication, down 2.3% over the previous 24 hours. The latest pullback followed a brief recovery to $1 on November 18, 2026, which proved short-lived as investors took profits, pushing the token back below the key psychological threshold.

Although VirtualBacon is not ready to accumulate XRP at current prices, he remains optimistic about its longer-term potential. The pundit believes the cryptocurrency could eventually reach $10, although he sees limited room for significant gains beyond that valuation.

A rally to $10 would represent a major milestone for XRP, pushing its market capitalization to approximately $635 billion based on a circulating supply of 63.52 billion tokens. This would place the cryptocurrency among the largest digital assets by market value and require its valuation to grow more than tenfold from current levels.

The projected price target also highlights the scale of growth XRP would need. At $10 per token, its fully diluted valuation, calculated using the maximum supply of 100 billion XRP, would reach approximately $1 trillion.

However, reaching such a valuation would require sustained demand, increased market participation, and significant capital flowing into the cryptocurrency. Notably, while VirtualBacon considers $10 achievable over the longer term, his outlook suggests that he sees this level as a more realistic upper target rather than expecting XRP to deliver substantially higher gains.

#5159. Palmer Luckey Says He’s Building an Offshore Haven for Industry—And 'Illegal' Medicine

Palmer Luckey Says He’s Building an Offshore Haven for Industry—And 'Illegal' Medicine

Palmer Luckey, the billionaire founder of Oculus and defense technology company Anduril, is spending over a billion dollars to have a private island where he can do things without worrying about U.S. laws.

He wants to build a massive industrial platform in the ocean where American businesses can operate without some of the regulations they face on land. And he has apparently been working on the idea for nearly a decade.

In an X post published Wednesday, Luckey revealed a project called SLAB, a proposed seastead with a 1,000-year design life that he says could support industries ranging from metal processing and oil refining to medical research and treatment.

Selling Oculus to Facebook in 2014 gave me money for helicopter flight lessons and developing a seastead called SLAB with a 1,000 year design life.

The goal was to be cheaper than land and absent state/local laws that cripple critical American industries like metal processing,… pic.twitter.com/aJkd0eOgLF

— Palmer Luckey (@PalmerLuckey) November 18, 2026

His goal, he wrote, is to create an industrial base cheaper than land and free from state and local laws that he believes are crippling critical American industries. Luckey described the project as a way to strengthen the United States rather than escape it, calling it a "relief valve" for outdated regulations.

“Me and a few close friends cashed it up at a $1B valuation back in 2017, and while it still has a long way to go, there has been significant progress towards my goal of reviving manifest destiny and westward expansion,” he wrote.

Luckey said he used proceeds from Facebook's 2014 acquisition of Oculus to finance the project.

The announcement included an AI-generated illustration of a gigantic offshore platform, rather than photographs of an operating facility so it’s hard to know which type of endeavor he’s dedicating his time and money to.

Luckey said he decided to disclose SLAB after learning that journalists were preparing to describe it as a floating AI data center. He rejected that characterization as incomplete, saying the project was considerably older and "way cooler than that."

Luckey provided a more specific indication of his ambitions in a heated exchange with journalist Joshua Topolsky, who questioned whether creating an offshore community was a sensible way to modernize medical research.

Topolsky argued that regulatory reform could happen without constructing an island in the ocean and challenged Luckey to identify which existing rules had prevented him from pursuing medical advances.

Luckey responded that he would rather build a place where people could save their families with "safe but currently illegal medicine ASAP" than wait for lawmakers to reform the system.

He accused political interests and large pharmaceutical companies of obstructing change, arguing that conventional reform would require legislation capable of securing support from 60 senators.

Luckey's plan also faces a significant legal complication: moving an operation into the ocean does not automatically place it outside American jurisdiction.

When another X user asked how close SLAB could operate to San Francisco without falling under its jurisdiction, Luckey answered, "Three miles."

That generally corresponds to the boundary of California's state-controlled coastal waters, according to the U.S. Bureau of Ocean Energy Management.

That said, federal jurisdiction extends considerably farther. The United States has a 12-nautical-mile territorial sea and a 200-nautical-mile exclusive economic zone, where it retains authority over certain economic activities and the construction and use of artificial islands, according to NOAA guidance.

The applicable rules would depend on SLAB's location, whether it floats or is attached to the seabed, and how it is registered. Even vessels operating on the high seas remain subject to applicable maritime laws and the jurisdiction of their flag states, so it’s not as if he’d be living in an anarchist fairy tale.

Medical research presents its own complications, if that’s what he’s actually doing. The FDA requires foreign clinical studies submitted in support of U.S. drug approvals to meet good clinical practice standards, including independent ethical review and informed consent. Conducting treatment offshore would not, by itself, make an unapproved drug legal for sale in the United States.

Previous seasteading experiments have encountered similar jurisdictional disputes. A floating community off Thailand triggered a confrontation with authorities in 2019, while a subsequent project in Panama adopted boat registration to operate within an existing legal framework.

This is all, of course, speculation. Luckey has not publicly identified SLAB's proposed location, the specific regulations it would avoid, or the medical treatments he hopes to make available. He has also not announced a construction deadline or provided independently verifiable details of the project's current physical development.

#5158. OmenX announces $2 million in funding from Informed Ventures and launches on-demand 30-second Bitcoin predictions

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Toronto, November 19, 2026 – OmenX, a Base-native leveraged prediction market platform, today announced $2 million in funding from Informed Ventures alongside the launch of its on-demand, 30-second Bitcoin price prediction product.

The new product lets users predict whether Bitcoin’s price will rise or fall over the next 30 seconds, with each trade starting its own prediction window. A position opened at 10:00:12 runs until 10:00:42. Users can act when their view forms, with a full 30-second horizon, rather than adapting to the remaining time in a shared market round.

Featuring zero-slippage execution and rapid settlement, the product extends the familiar five- and fifteen-minute crypto prediction format to a shorter, individually timed experience. It adds to OmenX’s intraday offering, which spans prediction horizons from 30 seconds to 24 hours, depending on the market.

Beyond crypto, OmenX offers directional prediction markets linked to more than 100 global assets, including U.S. equities, futures, ETFs and indices. Its event markets also cover live sports and esports, with sports coverage spanning major football leagues, basketball, American football, ice hockey, baseball and UFC.

Leverage has been central to OmenX’s platform from the outset, allowing traders to amplify their exposure on supported event markets. The latest launch builds on that foundation while advancing a broader goal: making prediction markets a practical financial tool for retail users.

OmenX organizes the trading decision around a defined question: will an asset’s price be higher or lower at the end of a specified period? Users can express a directional view without purchasing the underlying asset, targeting an exact closing price or constructing an options position that requires decisions about strikes, implied volatility and time decay.

“Leverage has been part of OmenX from the start, but our ambition goes beyond adding leverage to prediction markets,” said James, founder and CEO of OmenX. “We want to make global markets easier to participate in. A trader should be able to take a view on Bitcoin, a stock or a sporting event and express it through a clear contract and a timeframe that fits their judgment.”

The platform’s emphasis on short, recurring markets provides faster feedback on trading decisions and can reduce the time capital remains committed to individual outcomes. OmenX plans to expand its coverage of traditional and crypto assets while deepening the trading experience across supported markets.

OmenX is a Base-native leveraged prediction market platform covering crypto, global financial assets, live sports and esports. Through defined outcomes, short settlement cycles and leverage on supported markets, OmenX is building a more accessible way to trade market views.

Learn more at omenx.com. Media contact James Zhang

#5157. Near Protocol (NEAR), ChainLink (LINK) and Litecoin (LTC) Price Analysis For November 21: Market Gaining Strengh

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

NEAR Protocol, Chainlink and Litecoin remain under selling pressure as the broader cryptocurrency market continues to struggle. NEAR is testing support near $3 after losing much of its October gains, while LINK has fallen below the key $9 level as sellers maintain control.

NEAR Protocol ($NEAR) is trading near $2.98 after giving up some of its momentum from its October rally. Currently trading at $2.976 on Binance at 14:16 UTC on November 20, the asset has a day's high of $3.226 and a low of $2.962.

On its daily chart, NEAR has been on a steady decline since its October high near $6. Having given up much of its momentum from the September rally, NEAR has also been unable to sustain any of its rebound attempts. As such, the next price move will be critical for any potential recovery.

Currently trading below its short-term moving averages and testing its long-term moving average support near the psychologically significant $3 level, NEAR faces a key test. It remains to be seen whether this will lead to a potential rebound or further declines.

The market structure also remains bearish. However, lower trading volumes compared to the September rally point to weakening buying interest. Nonetheless, this does not necessarily confirm that selling pressure has eased.

Chainlink (LINK) has been on a steady decline, with each rebound attempt forming lower highs. Currently trading near $8.79 and below all of its major moving averages on the daily chart, LINK has also lost the important psychological support level at $9.

With its RSI approaching oversold territory, LINK faces another test as traders watch for signs of renewed buying interest.

For now, LINK/USD is trading at $8.786 at 14:16 UTC on November 20, after opening at $8.993, touching a high of $9.062, and hitting a low of $8.667.

Chainlink (LINK) is trading below all of its moving averages. In addition, it has dropped below the previous support at $9, indicating that selling pressure continues to control the market.

For LINK/USD to rise substantially from its current levels, LINK needs to reclaim the $11 area. Until then, the broader market outlook remains bearish.

Litecoin has reversed most of the bullish rally it experienced in September and is now facing renewed selling pressure. The cryptocurrency is currently hovering around $41.68.

Litecoin continues to form lower lows and lower highs since peaking near $75 in October.

Litecoin's volume has also failed to show any signs of increased buying pressure after the recent sell-off. Although green candles appear occasionally, they have not been strong enough to indicate a change in trend. For LTC/USD to reverse its current direction, it will need to show sustained buying pressure.

Selling pressure has remained strong throughout November, pushing the price below several support levels. At the time of writing (14:22 UTC on November 20), LTC is trading at $41.679, with a day's high and low of $42.454 and $41.047, respectively.

Overall, the daily chart remains bearish.

Currently, the price is trading below all the moving averages on the chart, while the short-term averages are also moving lower. This suggests that sellers remain in control.

Trading volumes have also failed to increase significantly, indicating limited buying interest in LTC. The RSI is close to the oversold region, meaning a short-term rebound cannot be ruled out.

That said, there is no confirmation of a reversal. Immediate support is at the $41 level, followed by the psychological $40 level.

A breakdown below $40 could result in further declines toward $38. For a turnaround, the price first needs to move above the 44-45 region. Therefore, the overall market bias remains bearish.

#5156. Bitcoin Below $62K After Anthropic IPO: Is a SpaceX Style Crash Coming?

Why is crypto down? Bitcoin broke $62,300 as stocks fell on Anthropic’s IPO day. After SpaceX, BTC bottomed 19 days later.

For traders wondering why crypto is down, the much-anticipated “post-ANT” rebound has turned into another disappointment. Bitcoin’s anticipated post-Anthropic IPO rally has yet to materialize. Instead, BTC slipped below $62,000 on Thursday, November 19, as US stocks fell roughly 2% on the same day the AI company made its Nasdaq debut.

Bitcoin closed at $61,987, marking its first daily close below $62,300 since July 8, before sliding further to around $61,800 on Friday morning.

BREAKING: MicroStrategy, $MSTR, and Bitmine, $BMNR, extend losses to their lowest level of the day as Bitcoin falls below $63,000.

The stocks are now down -88% and -91%, respectively, from their record highs. pic.twitter.com/BgxojZPDps

— The Kobeissi Letter (@KobeissiLetter) November 19, 2026

Anthropic itself had a strong but messy first day. Priced at $83 a share, it opened at $111.50, then faded to close at $97.17. The S&P 500 lost 2.0% and the Nasdaq Composite 2.3%, after the Nasdaq had opened higher.

SpaceX’s June listing is the closest comparison. We tracked Bitcoin’s path after that IPO day by day: it kept falling for almost three weeks before it bottomed.

So could Bitcoin repeat the SpaceX pattern, and when would the bottom come?

The listing was supposed to end the pressure. Bitcoin fell 22.7% in the 24 days before Anthropic’s debut, as traders sold crypto and other risky bets to make room for a mega-listing. Some, including Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse, expected that selling to stop once the shares started trading.

Instead, Thursday turned into a broad sell-off:

When tech stocks fall this hard, Bitcoin usually falls with them. That link is the simplest answer to why is crypto down this week.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Best New Crypto to Hodl

SpaceX listed on June 12. We lined up Bitcoin’s daily closes from 24 days before that day to 80 days after, and did the same for Anthropic.

So far, Anthropic is following the SpaceX script with one difference: it skipped the early bounce.

EXPLORE: Bitcoin Price Tumbles 19% Ahead of Anthropic IPO: Is SpaceX Crash Repeating?

Two listings are not a rule, and the backdrop has changed since June. For anyone asking why is crypto down and when it stops, here’s what lines up and what doesn’t.

If the SpaceX pattern holds, the worst could come in early December, followed by weeks of sideways trading. If stocks keep falling, the bottom could come sooner and lower.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Predicts Next Crypto to Hit 1 Dollar

On the daily chart, Bitcoin trades at $61,865. That is below its 50-day exponential moving average (EMA), an average that gives recent prices more weight, at $74,655, and its 200-day EMA at $75,470.

The next few weeks will show whether the SpaceX pattern holds, and the answer to why is crypto down may come from stocks more than from crypto. A bottom near $58,500 in early December, with stocks steadying, would look a lot like June. A break below $57,800 while stocks keep falling would mean this sell-off is bigger than one IPO.

#5155. Pulsar Enables USDT Deposits on TRON, Expanding Access to Everyday Stablecoin Payments

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Geneva, Switzerland, November 19, 2026 — Pulsar Money today announced support for USDT deposits on the TRON network, allowing users to fund their Pulsar accounts with USDT (TRC-20) and access everyday payment features through the app.

With this integration, users can deposit USDT on TRON and use their balances for online purchases, subscriptions, and other everyday expenses through the Pulsar virtual card. Pulsar charges no fees for supported stablecoin deposits across its plans, although network and third-party fees may apply.

The integration connects TRON’s widely adopted stablecoin infrastructure with Pulsar’s consumer-focused payment platform. In Q3 2026, TRON processed $2.21 trillion in stablecoin settlement volume, averaging $24 billion daily. By supporting USDT deposits on TRON, Pulsar offers users a convenient way to put their existing stablecoin holdings to use for everyday payments.

“Supporting USDT on TRON gives people another way to bring the stablecoins they already use into Pulsar,” said Alex, Founder and CEO of Pulsar Money. “Our focus is on making those balances useful in everyday life, whether people are managing their money, shopping online, or paying for subscriptions.”

“TRON’s extensive stablecoin activity reflects the growing demand for accessible, efficient digital payments,” said Sam Elfarra, Community Spokesperson for TRON DAO. “Pulsar’s integration brings that infrastructure closer to everyday use, expanding how people can access and spend their stablecoins.”

Pulsar combines stablecoin balance management and payment features in a single app. To deposit USDT on TRON, users can select the supported asset and network in the Pulsar app and follow the deposit instructions. Users should ensure they select TRON (TRC-20) and send only USDT to the deposit address provided for that network. Features and availability may vary by region and service provider.

Pulsar Money is a money app that brings stablecoins and everyday payment features together. Users can manage supported balances and access services including a virtual card and currency conversion, subject to availability and provider terms. Pulsar is not a bank.

Website | Download | X | LinkedIn | Instagram | TikTok | Blog | Telegram

TRON DAO is a community-governed DAO dedicated to accelerating the decentralization of the internet via blockchain technology and dApps.

Founded in September 2017, the TRON blockchain has experienced significant growth since its MainNet launch in May 2018. Today, TRON hosts the largest circulating supply of USD Tether (USDT) stablecoin, which currently exceeds $94 billion. As of November 2026, the TRON blockchain has recorded over 415 million in total user accounts, more than 16 billion in total transactions, and over $24 billion in total value locked (TVL), based on TRONSCAN. Recognized as the global settlement layer for stablecoin transactions and everyday purchases with proven success, TRON is “Moving Trillions, Empowering Billions.”

#5153. ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Grok Helped Plan Terror Attacks in Government Tests

ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Grok Helped Plan Terror Attacks in Government Tests

Four of the most widely used AI chatbots helped government investigators develop potentially deadly attack plans in less than 12 hours, despite safeguards intended to stop exactly that kind of request.

OpenAI's ChatGPT, Anthropic's Claude, Google's Gemini and Grok all supplied usable information during covert testing by the Government Accountability Office, according to a report released Wednesday. Investigators posed as attackers, then checked the responses against real locations, available materials, and expert assessments.

Two systems assisted with plans involving improvised explosives in crowded U.S. cities. Two others supplied information for attacks on electrical infrastructure, according to the watchdog, which withheld the models' assignments and operational details to prevent copycat attempts.

Senator Maggie Hassan, the New Hampshire Democrat who requested the investigation, warned that a terrorist could obtain "a concrete playbook for how to carry out a deadly attack" in less than 12 hours, according to her statement.

The chatbots initially activated protections, including refusing requests, removing harmful information or suspending accounts. Investigators bypassed those barriers with common jailbreak techniques, which manipulate an AI system into ignoring its safety rules.

The GAO checked suggested locations, researched whether materials were available and consulted specialists about the plans' feasibility. Some answers contained errors, including misplaced roads on a map, and investigators did not construct explosives or execute an attack.

In the report, "viable" means somebody would likely be able to follow the information supplied, not that an attack was demonstrated to succeed. Each model was tested on one assigned scenario, and the agency said the results were not statistically representative or suitable for ranking the companies.

OpenAI and Google said the tests involved older models, while Anthropic acknowledged that determined users can sometimes extract harmful material. The companies described continuing improvements to safety systems, though the GAO concluded those protections failed in its tests.

This is not really new. The findings follow a previous research showing eight of 10 popular chatbots assisted simulated teenage users planning violent attacks. The separate study tested 720 responses and found the bots provided actionable help roughly three-quarters of the time.

The GAO findings arrive amid outages affecting banks, Microsoft services and the National Security Agency. Officials say the disruptions were separate incidents, not evidence of a coordinated AI-driven cyberattack, even as the rush to repair software flaws has created its own problems.

Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton connected the NSA's outage to patching work. Officials described the recent breakdowns as maintenance-related, although individual organizations offered different explanations for their failures.

AI tools now find vulnerabilities faster than human teams can test and deploy fixes. Last month, the AI-assisted discovery of critical PHP flaws pushed website operators into an urgent patching campaign; the software powers a large share of the web.

The sheer volume is becoming another problem. VulnCheck data reveals there have been more than 100,000 software vulnerabilities logged this year by November 13, more than twice the total for 2025. Engineers still have to evaluate and deploy fixes without breaking the systems they are meant to protect.

That acceleration cuts both ways. Defenders gain powerful tools for finding weaknesses, but hasty repairs can disrupt essential systems, and malicious users may gain faster access to attack planning.

Hassan said she will make AI safety legislation an early priority when she takes over the Senate Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs Committee in January. She plans hearings examining risks to public safety and critical infrastructure; the GAO itself made no formal policy recommendations.

#5152. Uhm, Netflix, What Is This? CZ Slams The Altruists for Making Him the Villain Behind FTX’s Collapse

The Altruists by Netflix makes CZ the villain of FTX’s fall. Our FTT data shows the crash came after FTX asked Binance for help.

Binance founder Changpeng Zhao, known as CZ, has hit back at The Altruists Netflix series about the fall of FTX, which premiered on November 19. The show has found a villain for the story, and it is not the man serving 25 years in prison for it.

The Altruists Netflix release is a scripted drama, not a documentary, which may explain a few things. Its eight episodes follow Sam Bankman-Fried, played by Anthony Boyle, and his former girlfriend Caroline Ellison. Terry Chen plays CZ, cast as the rival who brought Bankman-Fried down.

There are forces spreading negative narratives, with massive amounts of funding behind it. https://t.co/Ymy4sA0cJd

— CZ 🔶 BNB (@cz_binance) November 20, 2026

The real story is hardly a mystery. A US jury convicted Bankman-Fried of fraud after a month-long trial, and a judge sentenced him to 25 years in prison. Three years on, Netflix has somehow reopened the question of who is to blame. So did CZ and Binance really sink FTX, as the show suggests?

We went back to the token prices from the week FTX fell to check.

The series, co-created by Graham Moore and Jacqueline Hoyt, is built around Bankman-Fried’s relationship with Ellison, who ran Alameda Research, FTX’s sister trading firm. Netflix’s own page for the show sums it up in one line: “Young, idealistic and suddenly worth billions, they tried to do good—but unleashed the worst in each other.”

Zhao is a supporting character, but a central one in the plot. The show has him setting a trap that pushes Bankman-Fried into front-running, which means trading ahead of customers’ orders to profit from them. Some viewers also read it as pressure on FTX to hand over trading data.

Critics have attacked the show on other points too. Constance Wang, FTX’s former chief operating officer, who is a character in the series, criticized its inaccuracies and its framing as a love story. Other critics say the focus on the couple’s romance downplays Bankman-Fried’s own responsibility for the fraud.

Market Intelligence: Crypto Analyst Reveals Upcoming Binance Listings

CZ did not write a long rebuttal. On the morning of November 20, he shared a Forbes commentary on X. The piece said The Altruists Netflix storyline turns him into a “crypto villain” and invents the front-running trap, and that there is no public evidence Zhao ever did such a thing.

He also posted a comparison image from March. In his words, it “already said it all.” Later that day he went further, saying that forces backed by huge financial support are spreading negative stories about him. He did not say who.

Yi He, Binance’s co-founder, was blunter. In a post in Chinese, she called the show’s characters thin and clichéd. “If you’re the protagonist, even your crimes are someone else’s fault,” she wrote. She compared it unfavourably with Hongguo, a Chinese short-drama app, and added: “Netflix might as well cancel it outright.”

Netflix and the showrunners have not responded publicly.

EXPLORE: BNB Crypto Drops 8.7% in an Hour as Binance Loses $6.36 Billion to Hackers, and Futures Traders Turn Short: Can BNB Hold Its 200-Day Average?

The real Binance did play a part in the final week. Here is the order of events in November 2022:

To see which of these moments broke FTX, we tracked FTT’s hourly price on Binance through that week.

Zhao’s post alone did not sink the token. FTT traded at $24.34 in the hour he posted it. Thirty-three hours later, at midnight UTC on November 8, it was still at $22.08, only 9% lower. Ellison had publicly offered to buy Binance’s FTT at $22, and the price held close to that.

The real crash came on November 8. FTT closed the hour of the deal announcement at $17.93, then hit $2.51 three hours later, a fall of 86%. That is when a run became a collapse: the rescue deal told the market that FTX could not pay its customers on its own.

Today FTT trades at about $0.17, down 99.3% from the hour of Zhao’s post.

A run like this only kills an exchange that does not hold its customers’ money. FTX did not. About $8 billion in customer deposits was missing, used to cover Alameda’s trading losses and spending.

At the 2023 trial, Ellison testified that Alameda had been taking FTX customer money for years, on Bankman-Fried’s instructions. The jury convicted him on all seven counts. The US Attorney’s Office in Manhattan announced in March 2024 that he had been sentenced to 25 years and ordered to forfeit about $11 billion. Ellison received two years.

Zhao has a criminal record of his own, from a separate case. He pleaded guilty in 2023 to failing to run an effective anti-money-laundering program at Binance and served four months in prison. Binance agreed to pay more than $4 billion, and President Trump pardoned him in October 2025.

Sam Bankman-Fried may be the UNLUCKIEST investor in history.

If FTX hadn’t SOLD its investments after the collapse, it would still hold:

Anthropic: $34.7 billion (69x)Robinhood: $4.3 billion (7x)Solana: $2.9 billion (1.1x)Cursor: $1.8 billion (9,000x)Sui: $600 million (6x)… pic.twitter.com/GdHj3Lldaw

— Coin Bureau (@coinbureau) November 16, 2026

The FTX estate has also gone after Binance. In November 2024 it sued Binance and Zhao for about $1.76 billion, saying the 2021 buyback of Binance’s FTX stake was paid with customer money. It also claimed his November 2022 posts helped set off the run. Binance has called the claims meritless.

So The Altruists Netflix version is right that Zhao’s post lit the fuse. It leaves out that the hole was there long before he posted, and FTT’s price only collapsed once FTX had to ask for a rescue.

#5151. What Happened the Last Time XRP Fell 28% in a Month

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. XRP has spent a week unable to get back above $1. In November 2026, the token hit a two-year low of $0.895, and on Nov. 20, it was trading near $0.96. XRP is down 27.7% in November and about 51% since January, according to CryptoRank data.

The last time XRP lost more than 28% in a month was February 2025, when the token fell 29.3%. That month, the crypto market was also hit by a $1.5 billion hack of the Bybit exchange on Feb. 21.

XRP did not recover right away. From March to June 2025, the token gained about 4.5% in total, according to CryptoRank. In July 2025, XRP jumped 35%, and on July 18, 2025, it set its all-time high of $3.65. On the same day, U.S. President Donald Trump signed the GENIUS Act, the country's first stablecoin law. XRP is now 73% below that record.

In November 2021, XRP lost 10%, and it fell another 17% in December and 25.6% in January 2022. In November 2022, the month of the FTX collapse, the token dropped 11.8%, and it lost 16.9% in December. XRP finished 2022 down about 59% and gained 20.1% in January 2023.

According to CryptoRank, XRP's median return is -10.2% in November and -4.07% in December.

SoSoValue data shows spot XRP ETFs have lost about $26 million to outflows since the start of November. About $19.1 million of that amount left the funds in the week ending Nov. 13.

On Nov. 19, Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse said on CNBC that investors had been selling crypto to buy shares in big listings. "People are taking liquidity out of a very liquid market, crypto included — Bitcoin, XRP — and moving it into SpaceX. The exact same thing happened coming into the Anthropic IPO. What we saw after the SpaceX IPO was a rally in the crypto markets. I think you'll see that happen here as well," he said.

#5149. Ledger Confirms Tampered Bitcoin Wallets in Berlin as Spy Implant Probe Spreads Beyond Asia

Ledger Confirms Tampered Bitcoin Wallets in Berlin as Spy Implant Probe Spreads Beyond Asia

The tampered Ledger wallets aren’t just a Southeast Asia problem anymore.

Ledger said Wednesday it has confirmed tampered hardware wallets in Berlin and is investigating reports of two more in Dubai, expanding a probe it had previously described as a localized incident in Southeast Asia.

“Our findings since then show tampered devices outside Southeast Asia. It is the same incident,” the company said in an update on its support site.

The investigation began last month after customers who bought devices from reseller CryptoBilis reported losses. Onchain investigators traced a suspected drain of about $87 million from hundreds of victim wallets across Ethereum, Tron, and Bitcoin.

Ledger said at the time that all confirmed cases were tied to CryptoBilis, and it later asked resellers in seven Asian markets to pull devices from sale.

Every device Ledger has confirmed shows the same kind of physical modification, designed to capture a user’s recovery phrase during setup. A recovery phrase, typically a string of 24 words, is the master key to a crypto wallet: anyone who has it can drain the funds.

Our investigation into the hardware implant incident we disclosed Oct 15th is ongoing, and we published an update: https://t.co/wx6mfQPr99

There is no flaw in Ledger's secure chip or operating system, and no confirmed case involving a device bought on https://t.co/vQ4QWa1D0f

Ledger maintains there’s no flaw in its secure chip or operating system and no confirmed case involving a device bought on ledger.com.

In Germany, Ledger said a few tampered units were localized to Berlin, and it urged anyone who bought a Ledger device there since January 2026 to contact support.

In Dubai, it’s working with distribution partners to learn how two suspected units entered circulation. Those devices were bought at a Virgin Megastore location, according to multiple reports. Ledger also found one affected Nano Gen5 among the Southeast Asian resellers it previously paused.

The company expects more tampered devices to surface, though it says the number remains small overall. Its Donjon security team is examining suspected devices, and Ledger has asked resellers to pause sales wherever inspections are ongoing.

Customers worried about a device should stop using it, Ledger said. It advises moving funds to a new wallet created on a device bought directly from ledger.com, without reusing the old recovery phrase or passphrase, since a tampered device can capture both. It also recommends filing a report with local authorities.

For a limited time, Ledger is offering physical inspections, including X-rays, at its factory in Vierzon, France.

“We will never ask for your recovery phrase,” the company said on X.

The last few months have been a rough stretch for hardware wallet markers overall. Rival manufacturer Trezor said this week that hackers who breached a third-party shipping partner likely took information tied to about 27,000 orders. That’s the company’s third such breach in as many months.

#5148. XRP on Track to Post Worst ETF Month Ever

The XRP ETF market might be bracing to close a record-breaking month of outflows as demand for the XRP-based investment product has remained on hold all through the month.

This comes as the prolonged and intense market volatility has extended to the ETF ecosystem, causing institutional investors to withdraw their capital.

Further data also shows that the spot XRP ETFs have yet to record a single day of net inflows throughout November, as the persistent price decline has caused investors to slow down their purchases.

As such, the influx of fresh capital into the spot XRP ETF market has remained on hold amid growing market uncertainty and a shift in investor sentiment.

#5147. Banks Are Adopting Stablecoins: Here Is Why Crypto-Native Dollars Still Win on User Freedom

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

As traditional giants rush into digital dollars, USDD’s Yvonne Chia explains why 24/7 access, self-custody, and transparent yield give decentralized stablecoins an edge banks can’t replicate.

20 November – Road Town, Totorla, British Virgin Islands – Decentralized stablecoin protocol USDD is accelerating its multichain utility across TRON, Ethereum, and BNB Chain—combining automated sUSDD yield via its Smart Allocator with GasFree zero-TRX transfers—as traditional financial giants race to build competing stablecoin rails.

In a recent industry interview with instant exchange partner ChangeNOW, USDD Head of Communications Yvonne Chia outlined why self-custody, 24/7 settlement, and transparent onchain reserves give decentralized digital dollars a structural advantage over bank-issued alternatives.

For billions of people globally, opening and maintaining a traditional bank account still involves paperwork, minimum balance requirements, geographic restrictions, and approval delays. Even when banks integrate stablecoin rails behind the scenes, those services remain gated behind legacy compliance walls and intermediary control.

Crypto-native stablecoins flip that model on its head. As Yvonne Chia highlighted during the interview:

“Compare this to a stablecoin like USDD, where all you need is a phone, a working internet connection, you can immediately start a wallet and you have access to your funds right away.”

With USDD, users can access and manage their digital dollars directly through their own wallets, without depending on traditional financial intermediaries.

This represents a fundamental shift in financial access: users are no longer limited to the services, operating hours, or account structures of a single institution. Instead, they have the freedom to hold, manage, and transfer their assets on their own terms.

Self-custody also gives users greater autonomy over their financial decisions. They can choose which wallets to use, where to transfer their assets, and which supported decentralized applications to interact with, without needing to route every transaction through a centralized financial institution.

With USDD, users’ digital dollars can move with them across an open blockchain ecosystem, rather than remaining tied to a particular bank or service provider.

Legacy payment networks still operate on decades-old schedules: weekend cutoffs, bank holidays, and multi-day cross-border settlement delays accompanied by hefty wire fees.

Blockchain networks never close. Whether it is a Sunday night or a public holiday, USDD moves globally without depending on traditional banking operating hours or settlement windows.

“We are able to have transfers 24/7 and we’re not limited to any traditional banking hours. So I think that in some ways, stablecoins are not just becoming banks, but exceeding what traditional banks can offer to users.”

To make everyday transfers even smoother than fintech apps, USDD introduced GasFree integration on TRON, allowing users on supported wallets to pay network fees directly in USDD without needing to hold TRX.

This removes another practical barrier to stablecoin adoption: having to acquire and maintain a separate gas token just to transfer digital dollars. With GasFree, eligible users can pay approximately 0.5 USDD per transaction, compared with around 1.5 USDT for a standard USDT transfer under the referenced fee conditions.

These improvements matter because the usefulness of a stablecoin is not determined by its peg alone. Low-cost transfers, straightforward wallet experiences, and around-the-clock availability are what make digital dollars practical for everyday payments and global value transfers.

Perhaps the sharpest contrast between banks and decentralized stablecoins lies in how yield is distributed.

In traditional banking, institutions take customer deposits, deploy them into yield-generating markets, and retain a significant portion of the returns. Everyday depositors may receive relatively low interest rates, while higher-yielding products often come with eligibility requirements, minimum deposits, or fixed maturity periods.

Stablecoins introduce another way to participate in digital-dollar opportunities. Rather than keeping capital entirely within a traditional deposit account, users can access onchain yield opportunities through decentralized protocols and transparent investment mechanisms.

USDD was built to return that value directly to holders. Through USDD Earn, users can deposit USDD to receive sUSDD, earning automated, competitive yield powered by our Smart Allocator system, with no fixed lock-up periods restricting access to their capital.

For users, the distinction is important: USDD Earn provides access to a productive stablecoin while maintaining flexibility, rather than requiring users to commit their capital to a conventional fixed-term product.

Traditional banks ask customers to trust closed-door balance sheets and periodic regulatory filings—until a crisis proves otherwise.

USDD takes a different approach through its “Don’t trust, verify” ethos, emphasizing publicly accessible onchain information and independent verification.

Transparency does not eliminate financial or technical risks, but it changes how users can evaluate them. Instead of relying exclusively on an institution’s assurances, users have access to information that can be independently examined and verified.

The bigger picture is an interconnected financial ecosystem in which users can move between holding, transferring, exchanging, and deploying their stablecoins according to their own needs. These are not isolated functions. Together, they make stablecoins more useful as everyday financial infrastructure.

Banks will undoubtedly continue using stablecoin rails to modernize their back-end settlement. However, true financial freedom comes from open networks where assets flow freely across protocols without intermediaries.

For USDD, the opportunity goes beyond offering a dollar-pegged asset. It is about making digital dollars accessible, transferable, and productive across an interconnected financial ecosystem. From self-custody and 24/7 transfers to flexible yield opportunities and multichain DeFi access, these capabilities give users more ways to put their money to work.

Banks may be bringing stablecoins into traditional finance. Crypto-native protocols are showing what stablecoins can make possible beyond it.

#5145. Why higher rates could slow Bitcoin buying at treasury firms

A treasury company can have years of dividends covered and still struggle to sell new shares on terms that make another Bitcoin purchase worthwhile.

Rising US interest rates could slow buying by Bitcoin treasury companies long before they have trouble paying their bills.

Strategy raised $244.7 million by selling common stock in the week ended Nov. 15. It spent $206.3 million of the proceeds buying back preferred shares and $33.4 million on 485 BTC.

At the time, its US dollar assets covered more than three years of preferred dividends and debt interest at current payouts. When Treasury bills pay more, investors can demand a higher yield to buy newly issued preferred shares.

For common stock, StoneX analyst Mark Palmer watches how much investors will pay above the value of the Bitcoin each share represents. At a premium, a company can issue stock and buy enough Bitcoin (BTC) to leave shareholders with more BTC per share. However, as that premium narrows, so does the gain.

#5143. 'Never a Dull Moment': Fidelity's Timmer Raises Stakes Around BTC's $62,000 Price Level

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Bitcoin fell to a fresh bear market low as widespread selling picked up across crypto, stocks and gold.

The Bitcoin sell-off deepened on Saturday, with the price reaching a low of $55,130, its lowest level since September 2024, extending a brutal run for risk assets. The latest sell-off deepens a bear market that began after Bitcoin peaked above $126,000 in October 2025.

In an X post, Fidelity Investments' Director of Global Macro, Jurrien Timmer, says he remains bullish on Bitcoin based on its four-year cycle and Power Law math. Timmer isn't oblivious to Bitcoin's recent poor performance, which he highlighted in a separate X post.

"I would be remiss if I didn’t mention Bitcoin and its very poor price action. It has gotten significantly more correlated to the S&P 500 in recent weeks, trading more like a speculative risk asset than a monetary asset," Timmer wrote.

Timmer added that Bitcoin has a dual personality in his own view, consisting of a monetary factor and a speculative factor. He depicted this by showing the rate of change in the GS non-profitable tech basket and the global money supply. When both are expanding rapidly, Bitcoin moons, and vice versa. Right now, risk appetites are receding as the AI theme corrects, while global liquidity is also receding, Timmer explained.

In his post, Timmer pointed out that Bitcoin is back on the Power Law support curve, which he says is a 'bit disheartening.' He highlighted the significance of this, saying that if Bitcoin’s price were ever to fall well below this support line, which is currently at $62,000 and rising, it would put his entire framework in doubt. "Never a dull moment!" he added.

I remain bullish on Bitcoin based on its four-year cycle and Power Law math, but it’s a bit disheartening that we are back again sitting on that power law support curve. If Bitcoin’s price were to ever fall well below this support line (currently at $62k and rising), it would put my entire framework in doubt. Never a dull moment!

Timmer's viewpoint seems to align with that of veteran trader Peter Brandt, who noted that Bitcoin might have entered a "dicey" price range owing to its recent drop. "The territory below this level gets very dicey," he said, underlining the $57,803 price point for Bitcoin.

In September 2026, Timmer pointed to a potential double-bottom formation near $60,000, noting that breaking past resistance near $80,000–$86,000 would support a push toward $100,000. Timmer also projects Bitcoin could reach $300,000 by 2029 based on a power-law model.

Bitcoin now trades well below $62,000, and this raises the question of whether the framework underpinning Timmer’s outlook still holds.

#5142. Bitcoin Struggles to Hold $64,000 as ETF Outflows Hit $2.9B in November

Bitcoin Struggles to Hold $64,000 as ETF Outflows Hit $2.9B in November

Bitcoin is struggling to hold the $64,000 level as investors continue to pull money from spot Bitcoin ETFs and brace for another interest rate hike from the Federal Reserve.

The leading cryptocurrency was trading at around $63,800 on Thursday, down 1% on the day and 13% over the past week, according to Decrypt data. It has hovered between roughly $63,000 and $65,000 since the weekend, briefly dipping below $63,000 and touching a 24-hour low of $62,068, per CoinGecko data.

"Bitcoin remains under selling pressure," Daniela Hathorn, senior market analyst at Capital.com, told Decrypt, noting that it has fallen roughly 26% from its late-September peak near $87,000. "Elevated Treasury yields, dollar strength and expectations of further Fed tightening are weighing on risk appetite, while the broader technology selloff has added to the pressure."

U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $306.4 million in net outflows on Wednesday, after $239.9 million on Tuesday, according to SoSoValue data. The funds have seen outflows on nine of the past 10 trading days, with Monday's small $8 million inflow the only exception, and have lost about $2.9 billion so far in November. Their total net assets have fallen to $77.7 billion. Bitcoin miners have added to the selling, with major miners including Riot and MARA selling some of their holdings to fund a shift into AI infrastructure.

Monday's brief inflow "does not signal a market inflection point," Tim Sun, senior researcher at HashKey, told Decrypt. Net short positions in Bitcoin futures held by leveraged funds on CME have fallen to their lowest level since early 2023, and futures basis yields have narrowed, he said, indicating that hedge funds have largely unwound the basis trade that paired spot ETF purchases with short futures. That means the current outflows are mainly driven by investors actively cutting their Bitcoin exposure, Sun said.

"If macro expectations do not improve, investors may shift from buying the dip to selling into rallies, creating additional overhead resistance on subsequent rebounds," he added.

"Recent Bitcoin ETF outflows and leveraged liquidations" have amplified the decline, Hathorn said. "Despite stronger Nvidia earnings, the market has struggled to stage a significant rebound, suggesting confidence remains fragile. A sustained recovery may require renewed ETF inflows and a more supportive interest-rate outlook."

Some traders have also pointed the finger at Anthropic's upcoming IPO for drawing money away from crypto, though analysts have disputed that the listing is draining crypto liquidity, attributing it instead to ETF outflows, forced selling and rising interest rates.

Traders see a 90.7% chance that the Fed will raise rates by a quarter point at its December 9 meeting, and a 9.3% chance of a half-point hike, according to CME FedWatch. The odds of a half-point move have risen from 6.5% a day earlier. Fed Governor Christopher Waller has called for an even bigger, half-point increase, unless policymakers signal another rise in January.

Hathorn said $64,000 is the immediate level to watch, followed by support around $62,000 and the psychological $60,000 mark. A sustained recovery above $66,000 to $68,000 could ease selling pressure, "but the broader trend remains bearish while lower highs persist," she said.

Onchain analytics firm Glassnode said in its weekly report on Tuesday that the crash had pushed investors’ realized losses to levels seen only near past market lows in 2018, 2020, 2022 and early 2023, a sign of capitulation. Bitcoin is now holding a dense block of recent buyers between $62,000 and $66,000, which grew by about 66,000 BTC during the two days of heaviest selling, Glassnode said. But a daily close below that block could set off a fast drop toward the June low of $58,500, and the Realized Price, the average price at which all Bitcoin last moved, at $53,700.

Traders on Decrypt Predict see a 43% chance that Bitcoin will fall to $60,000 at some point before the end of November, and a 12% chance that it will drop as low as $55,000. They give just a 2% chance that it will climb back to $82,500 this month.

There are some signs of support. About 1.1 million BTC, roughly 5.5% of supply, last changed hands at prices between $60,000 and $64,000, and that band has grown by more than 25,000 BTC in recent days, according to Neil Steinhardt, head of Nexo U.S., in a note shared with Decrypt Wednesday. A dense base of holders with costs near current prices "can act as a support zone, though it is not a floor," he wrote.

Bitcoin has also held up better than gold. The amount of gold one Bitcoin buys rose above its 200-day average this week, to about 17.1 ounces, around 40% above its March low, Steinhardt said.

#5141. Binance Futures Volume Hits Two-Month High Amid Prolonged Market Volatility

The world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange by volume, Binance is witnessing a rapid surge in its futures trading activity as the broader crypto market continues to face increased selling pressure.

While the market is still struggling with volatility and rapidly declining investor interest, the latest on-chain data from the crypto analytics platform CryptoQuant shows that futures traders have become extremely active on Binance.

The data shows that both Bitcoin and Ethereum have seen their perpetual futures trading volume on Binance surge to a combined high of $39.8 billion as of November 20.

Nonetheless, Bitcoin saw the highest increase in perpetual futures volume as it surged to $24.75 billion on the same day.

#5140. Ethereum’s Glamsterdam upgrade could slip to May 2027 over compatibility concerns

Ethereum developers are weighing changes to gas pricing proposals that could disrupt existing smart contracts, after contacting at least 50 affected companies.

Ethereum’s Glamsterdam upgrade could be pushed back as far as May 2027 if developers decide to modify its specifications to address smart contract compatibility issues, according to discussions at Thursday’s All Core Developers meeting.

Glamsterdam is a planned Ethereum hard fork aimed at increasing the network’s capacity and efficiency. However, blockchain security firm ChainSecurity warned in October that proposed changes to Ethereum’s gas pricing could disrupt existing smart contracts and potentially expose them to exploitation, prompting developers to postpone the Hoodi testnet fork, originally scheduled for Oct. 26.

The concerns center on gas repricing proposals EIP-8037 and EIP-8038. Researchers identified 13 real-world examples of potentially affected contracts in October, while a November investigation examined eight smart contract invariants that could be disrupted by the upgrade.

During Thursday’s meeting, developers said the Ethereum Foundation, ChainSecurity and security firm Dedaub had collectively contacted at least 50 affected companies. Three projects were classified as high impact, with fixes completed or expected by mid-December, while medium-impact projects reported timelines extending to Jan. 10.

Developers debated whether to retain Glamsterdam’s existing specifications or modify them to reduce compatibility risks, as changes intended to protect some contracts could disrupt others.

“Every spec change could, again, break new invariants that we are not aware of breaking yet,” one developer warned during the meeting.

Under the current specifications, mainnet activation could come as early as mid-January 2027. However, waiting until January to decide could push activation to late February or early March, or April or May if modifications are adopted, and no decision has been reached yet.

Related: Vitalik ‘pivots’ ETH to store of value, crypto suffers after US midterms: Hodler’s Digest

Separately, Ethereum’s next planned hard fork, Hegotá, could see its scope reduced as the network seeks to accelerate its transition to post-quantum cryptography.

During the meeting, developers considered postponing some Ethereum Improvement Proposals to prioritize post-quantum features, although several argued that reducing the upgrade’s scope would offer little benefit unless it also shortened the rollout timeline.

Developers also raised compatibility concerns over EIP-8151, a proposal designed to protect accounts migrating to post-quantum security. A preliminary review identified a potentially large number of existing smart contracts that could be affected by the changes, complicating the proposal’s inclusion in Hegotá.

No decision was reached on Hegotá’s scope, with client teams expected to submit their positions ahead of further discussions in two weeks.

#5139. XRP & XLM Hit Closest To ‘Value Area’ Among Major-Caps

Home » Altcoins » XRP & XLM Hit Closest To ‘Value Area’ Among Major-Caps

These two major-cap altcoin peers are hitting close to historically-undervalued territory: a highly-awaited bottom sign?

French analyst EGRAG compared the 4-hour charts of XRP, Ethereum, Hedera, Bitcoin, Solana, and Stellar to assess which assets are showing relative strength after recent lows.

The mainstream French crypto chart analyst’s review centers on price reaction, volume-profile location, and the distance remaining to each coin’s Value Area Low (VAL).

XRP is holding above its recent low without returning for a full retest. Solana is behaving in a similar fashion, maintaining its recovery structure. Stellar (XLM) is tracking XRP but appears slightly weaker. Ethereum (ETH) is retesting its lows, with a potential higher low developing.

Bitcoin (BTC) also retested and printed a slightly higher high. Hedera’s HBAR was retested with a lower high, the most shaky local structure among the six.

Distance to reclaim the VAL currently ranks as follows:

XRP and Stellar (XLM) are the nearest to re-entering their value areas. Bitcoin shows the more constructive local recovery structure among the larger-cap assets, even though it remains farther from its VAL than the two payment-focused names.

A double bottom is not confirmed simply because price holds above a prior low. The analysis treats bottoming as a process rather than a single candle. Early strength is marked by reclaiming the VAL.

#CRYPTO – 6 COINS, WHO IS SHOWING STRENGTH? #XRP – #ETH – #HBAR – #BTC – #SOL – #XLM FOLLOW THE PRICE, VOLUME & STRUCTURE! 👀The 4H charts reveal different reactions after the recent lows:▫️ #XRP: Holding above its recent low without a full retest.▫️ #ETH: Retesting the… pic.twitter.com/IFPBMuh7NJ

Structural confirmation requires a break of the local high. Stronger bullish conviction needs a reclaim of the Point of Control (POC) accompanied by volume.

The 4-hour volume profiles show each asset still trading below or near the lower boundary of its recent value area. Acceptance back inside the value area, followed by a move toward the POC, would shift the local bias.

Until that occurs, the charts remain in a recovery attempt rather than a confirmed reversal.

As of November 19, 2026, approximate prices stood near 63,700–64,000 for Bitcoin, $1,810 for Ethereum, $0.99 for XRP, $75 for Solana (SOL), $0.138 for Stellar (XLM) & $0.056 for Hedera Hashgraph (HBAR).

The broader market remains under moderate pressure, with Bitcoin dominance elevated and the Fear & Greed Index in Extreme Fear territory for consecutive days now.

The XRP coin & Stellar Lumens (XLM) are the nearest to early strength signals. Bitcoin’s slightly higher high on the retest is the more constructive structural note among the majors.

Confirmation across the group still requires a break of local highs and a return into the value area with volume. Until those conditions are met, the recovery remains unconfirmed.

#5138. Kraken Suffers Temporary Glitch Allowing Users to Buy Bitcoin for $16,900

Bitcoin reportedly became available at a massive discount on Kraken after a sharp dislocation in Kraken's dollar trading markets.

Users reported being able to purchase BTC for as little as 24,250 CAD, equivalent to $16,900 at that time.

Apparently, the incident has only affected the BTC/CAD trading pair on the platform, although some users claim the disruption appeared across several CAD markets.

The cause the glitch is still unclear, while the reported transactions are yet to be verified.

The incident might have involved thin liquidity problem in Kraken's CAD order books.

Large sell orders reportedly overwhelmed available buy orders, leaving insufficient demand to absorb the selling pressure.

According to some accounts, at one point about 20 BTC was being sold every 15 minutes, causing the imbalance to push BTC price against CAD further down.

One such transaction resulted in BTC price at around C$24,250, or $16,900. Compared with Bitcoin trading between $57,000-$58,000, the discount amounted to a staggering 71%. Other users reported BTC briefly trading around $27,000.

Kraken's status page continued to show its systems as operational during the incident. No official explanation from the exchange was available at the time of writing.

The reported dislocation highlights the risks associated with trading in markets with limited liquidity. When an order book lacks sufficient buy-side depth, even relatively large sell orders can cause prices to move sharply away from the broader market.

On Friday, Bitcoin price dropped below $60,000. At some point, it fell 5.10% to $55,830, the lowest level since September 2024.

Liquidations on crypto exchanges reached $1.57 billion over 24 hours on Nov. 20.

According to CoinGlass, 265,525 traders were liquidated over 24 hours, and long positions accounted for $1.43 billion of the total.

Bitcoin saw $554.28 million in liquidations, and 91.3% of that amount came from long positions. Ethereum (ETH) fell below $1,700 on Nov. 20, and ETH traders saw $331.08 million in liquidations.

Binance accounted for 45.8% of all liquidations, and the largest single order was also on Binance: an $11.98 million ETH/USDT long position that was liquidated at $1,638.78.

#5137. Hyperliquid traders bet on Anthropic after platform ends up on Hong Kong’s warning list

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hong Kong has just put Hyperliquid [HYPE] on its watchlist. And yet, traders don’t seem to be bothered.

In fact, on Hyperliquid itself, the action is only heating up now.

On Wednesday, Hong Kong’s Securities and Futures Commission (SFC) added Hyperliquid to its Alert List. The problem cited was that they don’t hold a licence to operate in the city.

The regulator’s entry covers two names; Hyperliquid and Hyperliquid Hong Kong. It also shared the exchange’s trading website. It is believed that the platform may be serving Hong Kong users, presenting itself as a local operator, or pitching its services to people in the city.

With this addition, the SFC’s list of suspicious crypto trading platforms has grown to 69 names now. At the time of writing, Hyperliquid hadn’t commented on the development.

Back in June, Singapore’s central bank placed Hyperliquid on its Investor Alert List which flags firms people might wrongly assume are regulated there. At the time, Hyperliquid’s response was simple — it never said it had a Singapore licence.

Now, there are two Asian financial hubs keeping an eye on the exchange.

While Hong Kong was flagging Hyperliquid, traders on the platform were busy betting on Anthropic.

On 18th November, the number of traders holding long positions on Anthropic perps hit a record 5,567.

A week ago, that figure was 2,926; nearly doubling in seven days. There were 1,996 short holders, with the same hinting at 2.8 longs for every short.

Most of that action was on Trade[XYZ]’s ANT market. According to Token Terminal, this alone accounted for 4,190 long holders,.

One caveat, though. These numbers count traders, not the size of their bets. And since the data adds up three markets, anyone trading on two of them gets counted twice.

The altcoin was trading at $67.66, at press time, following a hike of just 1% in the last 24 hours. Its price was still a long way from its late-October levels when it briefly climbed above $95. Since then, it’s been mostly downhill for the crypto.

There seemed to be some sign of a breather though. The RSI was below 30 during that drop. It has since come back to just nudging above its average.

The CMF at -0.06 suggested that sellers still had the upper hand, but the outflows were clearly weaker than earlier this month.

#5136. GameStop CEO Says Digital-Only Games Have ‘Hurt Consumers’ as GTA 6 Skips Discs

GameStop CEO Says Digital-Only Games Have ‘Hurt Consumers’ as GTA 6 Skips Discs

GameStop CEO Ryan Cohen has criticized video game publishers for abandoning physical discs, as Grand Theft Auto 6, one of the most anticipated games ever made, launches on Thursday as a digital download only.

"When you buy a game, you should be able to sell it, trade it or lend it to a friend," Cohen told Bloomberg. "Unquestionably, the decision to move to all digital has hurt consumers, and it's also hurt GameStop."

Cohen declined to name specific companies, but Take-Two Interactive is releasing the $80 game only as a download, with retailers selling boxes containing download codes. Asked about presales, Cohen said: "It's a code in a box. I mean, we're selling a lot of it."

Take-Two declined to comment to Bloomberg.

The shift hits a core part of GameStop's business. Pre-owned and refurbished products made up 22% of the retailer's sales, about $171 million, in the quarter that ended August 1, down 32% from a year earlier, according to Bloomberg.

Cohen said he has spoken with publishers, who he argued benefit from limiting trade-ins because used copies compete with new sales. "They're taking away competition," he said.

Sony announced in July that it will stop producing physical discs for new PlayStation games in January 2028, a move experts have said will hit low-income gamers hardest. PlayStation founder Ken Kutaragi has dismissed the backlash as a "mentality issue," while an Infinity Ward veteran said publishers have pushed to cut discs since 2007.

Cohen said he has met with Rep. Ritchie Torres (D-NY), who is backing the Power to the Players Act, which would require gaming companies to give consumers ongoing access to the titles they buy. "A game that can be taken away the moment a company pulls the plug is not a purchase. It is a rental disguised as a sale," Torres said in October.

In Europe, the Stop Killing Games campaign gathered 1.3 million signatures for a European Citizens' Initiative calling for publishers to keep games playable after support ends.

The question of whether buyers own digital games has also reached the courts. Sony has argued in court that digital games can't be owned, prompting some in the crypto industry to pitch blockchain-based ownership as a fix.

Cohen's GameStop has also made bets beyond gaming. The company bought 4,710 BTC for more than $500 million in 2025, and in March moved all but one of them into a covered call strategy on Coinbase Prime to generate income. In May, after eBay rejected its takeover offer, GameStop sought shareholder approval to raise its authorized share count to 2.5 billion as it built a stake in the online marketplace.

#5134. Bitcoin’s $700M liquidation wave deepens – Is crypto’s $2T market cap at risk?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Anthropic’s post-IPO performance appears to have created a volatile market setup.

On the technical side, ANT opened at $111.50, around 34% above its $83 IPO price, before losing much of its early gains and closing at $97.17. This shows that initial demand was strong, but profit-taking quickly kicked in. The impact also seems to have spilled over into crypto, with the total market cap forming its third consecutive lower low at $2.08 trillion, a level last seen in early Q3.

Naturally, this raises a question: Is crypto’s weakness simply a result of ANT-driven volatility? Interestingly, recent Glassnode data points to a possible link. As the chart below shows, Anthropic futures on crypto platforms recorded $9.5 billion in trading volume ahead of its Nasdaq debut. In comparison, SpaceX, the largest IPO on record, attracted $3.9 billion in pre-listing futures volume before its June debut.

In essence, ANT’s pre-IPO futures generated more than 2.4x SpaceX’s.

This highlights how much larger speculative interest in ANT was compared to SpaceX, setting a new record for pre-IPO trading activity on crypto platforms. However, the initial hype quickly turned into profit-taking, with ANT dropping 13% from its opening price. This shift in sentiment may have added to Bitcoin’s [BTC] selling pressure, especially as liquidation activity picked up alongside the broader market breakdown.

So, is Bitcoin’s latest breakdown the start of an overall deleveraging process in crypto markets? In other words, could IPO-driven volatility have accelerated long liquidations, forced overleveraged traders out of positions, and triggered a general market reset?

It looks like ANT’s futures activity on crypto platforms may have added to the selling pressure.

According to CoinGlass, over $700 million worth of positions have been liquidated in the last 24 hours. Of that amount, nearly $600 million was long positions, indicating that leveraged bulls have been badly hit by the correction. However, can this flush-out in Futures markets lead to a rebalance?

Worth mentioning here is the ongoing liquidity drain. In the last 30 days, $11.5 billion has left the stablecoin supply. That outflow is greater than it was in the aftermath of the Terra collapse in 2022. While many in the crypto market are looking at Bitcoin’s price movements, the real problem is the decreasing capital in crypto.

Why does this matter? Lower stablecoin supply means less available liquidity to support buying activity, which could keep pressure on Bitcoin and the broader market.

So, while ANT-related volatility may have contributed to market deleveraging, liquidity is still very poor for a sustained recovery. In this context, ANT’s IPO may have added to an already fragile market, while concerns around its valuation may also “continue” to create a risk-off sentiment in U.S. markets.

Against this backdrop, a drop in the total crypto market cap below $2 trillion cannot be ruled out.

ANT’s IPO may have added to Bitcoin’s fear and increased crypto selling.

Weak liquidity could keep pressure on crypto, putting the $2 trillion market cap at risk.
