#5133. Binance Issues One-Letter Warning to Crypto Users Over Fake Websites

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Crypto exchange Binance has issued a "one-letter" security warning to crypto users, urging them to carefully check website addresses before logging into their accounts. According to Binance, a fake site can be just one letter off.

A post on X by the crypto exchange pointed out that a small difference in a website URL, sometimes just one letter, can lead users to visit fake websites impersonating legitimate platforms.

"Sometimes the biggest red flag is just one letter," Binance wrote.

Sometimes the biggest red flag is just one letter. Before you log in, double-check the URL. If you’re still not sure, use our official verification tool below 👇 https://t.co/HT068SANmn https://t.co/pBOQeU8GmC

The exchange advised users to double-check URLs before entering their login credentials. It also encouraged anyone uncertain about a website's authenticity to use Binance's official verification tool to confirm whether the URL belongs to the platform.

The Binance Verify platform allows crypto users to stay safe from scammers, as they can enter a URL, email address, phone number, Telegram account, or social media account to check if the source is verified and officially from Binance.

Impersonation scams are quietly growing across the crypto space, with one of the most troubling tactics seen being the deepfake: lifelike, AI-generated impersonations of crypto executives and employees soliciting money on video calls that look and sound real enough to make anyone hesitate. The personas are fabricated, but the sophistication is genuine.

Every day, people are contacted by impersonators using the names, likenesses, and even job titles of crypto personnel, including Binance staff, to solicit investments or financial transactions via WhatsApp, Telegram, and other channels.

Binance stated that scammers prey on people's growing interest in investing and digital finance and on their trust, often masquerading as its employees or executives to exploit its credibility.

With advances in AI and deepfake technology, they can create personas that look and sound real. Crypto users are therefore urged to beware of unsolicited advice or "special opportunities" and to refrain from clicking on unknown links or URLs.

#5132. Why Bitcoin Just Dropped to a 5-Month Low Near $61,300

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Why Bitcoin Just Dropped to a 5-Month Low Near $61,300

Three separate market pressures just collided—pushing BTC to a level not seen since July.

Bitcoin plunged 4.35% over the past 24 hours, falling to $61,300 — its lowest level since early July — as a global sell-off in AI stocks, heavy withdrawals from Bitcoin ETFs and a wave of forced liquidations sent shockwaves through crypto markets.

By Friday morning, Bitcoin was struggling to stay above $61,500, putting it on track for its seventh consecutive weekly decline.

This drop wasn’t triggered by a single event. Instead, several pressures hit the market at once: growing concerns over AI valuations, institutional investors pulling money out of Bitcoin ETFs, and leveraged traders being forced to close their positions as prices fell.

The broader pullback was driven by a retreat from riskier assets as investors questioned lofty AI valuations.

On Thursday, November 19, AI company Anthropic made its highly anticipated debut on the Nasdaq. Shares opened at $111.50 against an IPO price of $83, briefly pushing the company’s valuation above $2 trillion. However, the successful IPO failed to lift sentiment across the technology sector.

As Anthropic began trading, Bitcoin fell from around $62,700 to $62,250 within roughly 30 minutes, while broader Nasdaq losses accelerated.

By the end of the session, the Nasdaq was down 2.3%, the S&P 500 had fallen 2%, and the Dow Jones lost 2.2%.

The sell-off spread to Asia, where South Korea’s KOSPI dropped approximately 5%, triggering a temporary restriction on automated selling.

Institutional demand for Bitcoin is also showing signs of weakening, adding further pressure to an already struggling market.

According to SoSoValue data, U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $582.19 million in net outflows on November 19, marking the largest single-day withdrawal of the month.

With the exception of a modest $8 million inflow on November 5, U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs have faced nearly two weeks of persistent capital withdrawals, signaling a significant pullback in institutional demand.

As spot prices broke below key support levels, leveraged derivatives traders fueled a cascade of forced selling.

CoinGlass data shows $697.35 million in crypto positions were liquidated over the past 24 hours. About $600M came from long positions, and $201.25M of the total was Bitcoin longs.

According to market analyst Axel Adler Jr., Bitcoin remains under significant bearish pressure, with short-term investors sitting on substantial unrealized losses. Bitcoin is currently trading 15.7% below the average cost basis of recent buyers and has remained below that level for 12 consecutive days.

Until BTC reclaims the short-term holder cost basis and U.S. spot ETF flows flip back into positive territory, an analyst warns that the broader market correction isn’t over yet.

Bitcoin’s slide alongside AI stocks underscores how tightly crypto still tracks risk appetite in traditional markets. Continued ETF outflows and leveraged liquidations are likely to keep pressure on prices until broader market sentiment stabilizes.

#5131. Trump’s SEC nominee Johnson backs formal rulemaking for tokenized stock trading

Former CFTC Commissioner and Democrat Kristin Johnson and ex-SEC aide Steven Levine appeared before lawmakers Thursday.

President Donald Trump’s nominees to fill two vacancies at the US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) faced questions about investor protections, regulatory oversight and financial innovation during a Senate confirmation hearing Thursday, including scrutiny of the agency’s approach to tokenized securities.

Kristin Johnson, Trump’s Democratic nominee, backed formal rulemaking to address concerns about the agency’s exemption for tokenized stock trading.

“I feel strongly that regulation is made better through the notice and comment period,” Johnson told the Senate Banking Committee in response to Democratic Senator Angela Alsobrooks. She added that she would welcome the opportunity to address issues covered by the exemption through that process.

The SEC’s Sept. 17 exemption lets qualifying platforms trade blockchain-based versions of US-listed shares without registering as securities exchanges under certain conditions. The order took effect immediately and invites comments on possible changes.

Johnson, a former Commodity Futures Trading Commission commissioner, appeared alongside Republican Steven Levine, a former aide to SEC Commissioner Mark Uyeda. If confirmed, they would bring the five-member commission to four members, with three Republicans and one Democrat.

The hearing comes as the SEC has scaled back enforcement activity and pursued regulatory changes intended to accommodate digital assets, including efforts to facilitate tokenized securities that have drawn scrutiny from lawmakers and market participants.

The measure has drawn objections from publicly traded companies, with 67 opposing the tokenization of their shares on the OKXICE platform, according to a Nov. 5 public notice.

Johnson also cited concerns about Anti-Money Laundering and Know Your Customer compliance, noting that objections to the exemption extended beyond investor protection.

Levine, meanwhile, said he had not closely studied the innovation exemption but understood it to be time-limited and include investor protections. He emphasized that Congress had authorized the SEC to grant exemptions from federal securities laws when doing so serves the public interest and remains consistent with investor protection requirements.

He pointed to exchange-traded funds as an example of financial innovation that initially developed through regulatory exemptions before becoming subject to broader agency rules.

Related: Robinhood’s Tenev warns US tokenization uncertainty deters ‘serious assets’

If confirmed, Johnson would become the SEC’s only Democratic commissioner, a development some in the crypto industry believe could strengthen the agency’s approach to regulation.

“Crypto isn’t a partisan issue, and the rules that come out of a bipartisan commission will stand on firmer footing,” Jessica Martinez, US policy director at digital asset infrastructure company Fireblocks, told Cointelegraph.

Martinez added that even minority commissioners can influence policy by challenging proposals and pushing for unresolved issues to be addressed before rules are finalized.

Senators have until noon Friday to submit additional written questions to the nominees, with responses due by 5:00 pm ET on Nov. 24, according to the committee’s closing remarks.

The nominations could then advance to a committee vote before being considered by the full Senate. Both nominees would require Senate confirmation before taking their seats at the SEC.

#5129. Binance Wins $6.5B Freezing Order Over Hack That Can Be Served via the Blockchain

Binance Wins $6.5B Freezing Order Over Hack That Can Be Served via the Blockchain

Binance has secured a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing order against the hackers behind last month's attack on the exchange, which the FBI has blamed on North Korea.

The injunction was issued by the courts of the Abu Dhabi Global Market (ADGM) against the unknown perpetrators, Binance's lawyers at Gibson Dunn confirmed, according to City AM.

The order breaks new legal ground. It can be served directly via the blockchain, and market participants will be notified of a "living list" of 26,000 wallet addresses identified as holding stolen assets, Gibson Dunn partner Kendall Day said. That will enable "those assets to be frozen rapidly even as they are laundered at pace," Day said.

Binance has also secured proprietary injunctions and delivery-up orders, including against parties holding frozen assets who are not accused of wrongdoing, according to City AM. Decrypt has reached out to Gibson Dunn for comment.

"We will continue using every lawful tool available to trace, freeze and recover stolen assets, support our partners in returning funds where possible, and keep those assets out of the hands of the attackers," Dugan Bliss, Binance's deputy general counsel, said in a statement reported by Bloomberg.

The FBI has attributed the October 29 hack to TraderTraitor, a group linked to North Korea, which has denied involvement. The attackers gained access after an engineer was phished through a fake job offer, according to Bloomberg. The Secret Service has joined the FBI's investigation.

About $2.85 billion in funds connected to the hack has been frozen, including by Ripple and World Liberty Financial, according to Bloomberg. Stablecoin issuers Tether and Circle froze $2.7 billion shortly after the attack, and the Justice Department said last week it had obtained court authorization to seize or restrain $1.2 billion of that.

The hackers have used thousands of wallets and multiple blockchains to launder the rest, and wallets linked to the hack began moving Ethereum again this week after two weeks of inactivity.

Binance has said it fully covered the $6.3 billion in customer assets affected and absorbed the losses itself.

The daily burn rate has dropped by 37.21% due to a burn of 303,294 SHIB worth just $1 over the last day.

157.31 million SHIB were burned in the last seven days, with the burn rate increasing by 113%; a different scenario presents itself on a 30-day basis, when the monthly burn rate fell 48.87% with 343.11 million SHIB burned.

SHIB is exhibiting a strong accumulation of buy orders, which could signal a key support level as traders monitor buyers’ ability to soak up selling pressure.

A buy wall occurs when a lot of limit buy orders are stacked up around some price level and can show strong buying interest, providing temporary support if market participants are willing to fill those orders.

Now, if the price of Shiba Inu goes lower, then the 17.92 trillion SHIB order concentration that was reported could be an important area to watch. If the orders get filled and remain there, they could help absorb some of the selling pressure.

#5127. Bitcoin institutional holdings hit $29.78B – What changed in Q3?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Things have changed dramatically since the third quarter of 2026, when institutional interest in Bitcoin [BTC] increased. After three quarters of net selling, institutional investors resumed net purchases of spot Bitcoin exchange-traded products (ETPs).

According to Alex Thorn from Galaxy Research, institutions added approximately 49,229 BTC-equivalent during the three-month period.  Notably, by the 30th of September, institutional investment managers held a record 356,491 BTC-equivalent, worth approximately $29.78 billion.

The growing role of financial advisors and wealth management platforms played a major role.

Together, they held 144,307 BTC-equivalent by the end of September, representing 40.5% of all Bitcoin ETP holdings reported by institutional managers. If looked back, their holdings have increased for nine consecutive quarters and are now 3.3 times higher than their March 2024 levels.

That being said, large banks played a major role in this accumulation, with Wells Fargo, Bank of America, and Morgan Stanley being among the biggest buyers during Q3.

Wells Fargo alone added 14,636 BTC-equivalent, taking its total holdings to 30,553 BTC-equivalent, making it the largest institutional holder of U.S. spot Bitcoin ETPs, overtaking Jane Street.

Institutional ownership also shifted, with hedge funds dropping from 50.6% in December 2024 to 19%, while financial advisors and wealth platforms increased their exposure. Of this, BlackRock’s IBIT accounted for 65.2% of institutional Bitcoin ETP holdings.

That said, Bitcoin ETP holdings fell by 38,531 BTC between the 30th of September and the 16th of November.

However, the tokenized ETP sector continued to struggle, with its market capitalization falling 6.2% to $595 million and trading volume declining to $160 million, as previously reported by AMBCrypto.

This comes as Bitcoin spot ETFs recorded net inflows of $2.65 billion in September 2026 and $3.52 billion in August, while July saw smaller inflows of $172.43 million. However, the trend reversed in October, with $515.03 million in net outflows, followed by significantly larger outflows of $3.47 billion in November 2026.

Home » Altcoins » Solana (SOL) » GTA VI Launch Sparks $250M Solana Stock Frenzy Over Nvidia

Tokenized Take-Two shares saw a massive 24-hour trading spike as on-chain markets attract Washington scrutiny.

The long-awaited release of Grand Theft Auto VI didn’t just break gaming records—it briefly turned publisher Take-Two Interactive into the hottest stock on the blockchain, outselling even AI giant Nvidia.

On November 18, decentralized exchange (DEX) trading volume for tokenized Take-Two shares (TTWO) exploded by 578%, jumping from $36.8 million to $249.5 million in a single day, according to Token Terminal data.

That was roughly 2.8 times the $88.1 million traded in tokenized Nvidia (NVDA) shares over the same 24-hour window, accounting for 42% of all on-chain stock trading.

Nearly all of the action, roughly $239 million, or 96% of total volume, happened on Solana via Backpack Securities, a subsidiary of crypto exchange Backpack. Launched earlier this year, the token is designed to be backed 1:1 by real Take-Two shares held in custody.

Traditional Wall Street traders saw a much calmer reaction: Take-Two’s Nasdaq shares rose 6.2% to close at $216.61 on launch day before pulling back 3.7% to $208.65 the next session.

Despite Take-Two’s one-day viral spike, Nvidia remains the undisputed heavyweight of tokenized equities, boasting a $399.4 million market cap and $2 billion in 30-day trading volume (compared to Take-Two’s $808.1 million).

Overall, tokenized public stocks on Solana have nearly doubled since late October to reach $1.22 billion.

That rapid growth is drawing fresh scrutiny in Washington. On November 16, four Democratic senators sent a letter to the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) questioning how its newly introduced exemption for tokenized securities would address investor protection and anti-money laundering requirements.

The issue also surfaced during the November 19 Senate Banking Committee confirmation hearing for Kristin Johnson, President Donald Trump’s nominee for the SEC. Johnson indicated that exemptions covering tokenized stocks should eventually be formalized through rules following a public comment period.

The TTWO spike shows tokenized stocks can attract event-driven trading on-chain, comparable to some of the largest names in the segment. It also coincides with growing regulatory scrutiny of how tokenized stocks are supervised.

#5125. Cardano Lifeline? 3.96 Million ADA Buy Wall Emerges on Binance

The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Cardano fell to $0.133, a five-month low on Saturday as the crypto market sell-off deepened. At the time of writing, Cardano had slightly rebounded, up 1.82% in the last 24 hours to $0.144, as a substantial buy wall of 3.96 million ADA emerged on Binance, offering a layer of support as sellers pressured the cryptocurrency.

A buy wall occurs when a large volume of buy orders is concentrated around a particular price level on an exchange's order book. Such an order cluster can indicate that traders are prepared to purchase an asset at that price, potentially slowing further declines if selling pressure meets sufficient demand.

In Cardano's case, the 3.96 million ADA order cluster that occurred below the $0.14 level revealed a notable level of buying interest. As the orders at this level were filled, they helped absorb selling pressure for Cardano and provided short-term support for the price.

Current data from CoinGlass shows that open interest is down 1.98%, mostly due to shorts closing, although funding remains negative at −0.0099%, with shorts paying longs.

The next key levels to watch for ADA, according to on-chain data provided by CoinGlass, are $0.1462, which has a short liquidation cluster, and $0.1375, which has a long liquidation cluster.

Dijkstra hard fork preparations continue to progress, with Intersect, a Cardano-based entity, revealing what might come next in the coming weeks.

in about one or two weeks, the Cardano Node 11.2.3 release (GitHub), the Node RC 2026w47 release for the Musashi network (GitHub), and the publication of v6 of CAP-12 to the Constitutional Amendment Portal are anticipated.

In about two to three weeks, the Cardano Node 11.3 pre-release is anticipated, while an update to the Constitution governance action on-chain is expected in about one month.

Dijkstra requires both constitutional and technical changes in the era of on-chain governance. These include Constitutional guardrails (Update Constitution) and Plutus V4 (Parameter Update).

CAP-12 "Dijkstra era parameters" proposes to amend the Constitution to accommodate new block type definitions and set guardrails for new updatable parameter types introduced with Protocol Version 12.

#5124. Michael Saylor prioritizes STRC buybacks as Strategy’s Bitcoin buying slows 90% – Why?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Nothing highlights the impact of ongoing market volatility better than Strategy.

In a recent interview, Michael Saylor outlined a potential shift in Strategy’s approach. He explained why the company could sell MSTR shares even below its net Bitcoin value to buy back STRC. The key takeaway? The market is calling this arguably the most revealing part of the 19th of November’s investor Q&A.

Fundamentally, this seems logical. As outlined below, MSTR has performed extremely poorly as Bitcoin has fallen, losing over 40% of value within Q4. If MSTR loses another 25% of value, it will have lost more this quarter than last year’s Q4 crash, potentially making it the worst quarter MSTR has had since the bear market back in 2022.

The result? Strategy’s Bitcoin [BTC] accumulation has slowed sharply in Q4 2026.

In Q4 2025, Strategy bought 32,470 BTC. In comparison, it has only bought 3,183 BTC so far in Q4 2026. This represents a drop of 90%. To date, Strategy also used $206.3 million proceeds from selling MSTR shares to buy STRC shares. However, only $33.4 million has been used to buy Bitcoin.

In short, broader market weakness is putting Strategy under pressure. Against this backdrop, Michael Saylor’s latest STRC strategy doesn’t look like a random move. Strategy seems to be shifting more capital toward supporting its preferred stock while navigating a difficult market. But with FUD around the company continuing to build, the bigger question is whether STRC can still attract enough investor demand to support Strategy’s Bitcoin accumulation plans.

One thing is clear: Strategy’s main priority is to keep STRC stable.

As one analyst pointed out, this could mean the company would continue selling MSTR shares to fund STRC buybacks. If that isn’t enough, Strategy could also sell Bitcoin whenever STRC trades below its $100 par value. Saylor believes STRC’s 12% dividend is already attractive enough, making it a key income-generating product for institutional investors.

But what does this mean for Strategy shareholders? As the chart below shows, major institutional investors increased their MSTR holdings in Q3 2026. The top 15 now own around 33% of all outstanding shares. This shows that big investors are still interested in MSTR despite market weakness.

However, with Strategy focusing more on STRC, will MSTR shareholders still benefit from its BTC strategy?

Notably, this is where a potential feedback loop comes in. Continued weakness in MSTR could push Strategy to rely more on STRC buybacks to support its capital structure. However, this could also put institutional demand for MSTR at risk. If investor confidence weakens, raising fresh capital for future STRC buybacks could become more difficult.

So, for now, Saylor’s shift towards STRC signals a “risky” change in how Strategy plans to fund future BTC purchases.

Strategy is focusing more on STRC buybacks as MSTR struggles, potentially slowing Bitcoin purchases.

STRC’s 12% dividend may attract investors, but can it maintain demand without hurting MSTR?

#5123. Hyperliquid Anthropic futures priced within 1% of Nasdaq opening price

Hyperliquid traders priced Anthropic futures within 1% of the stock’s Nasdaq opening price, as trading surged ahead of the AI company’s IPO.

Anthropic perpetual futures on Hyperliquid were priced within 1% of the company’s Nasdaq opening share price Thursday, shortly before the stock began trading.

Anthropic shares opened at $111.50, up 34.3% from the company’s $83 initial public offering (IPO) price, according to several data dashboards. The opening price valued the Claude AI chatbot maker at around $2 trillion.

Just before trading began, Anthropic futures on Hyperliquid’s trade.xyz market were priced at $110.48, according to Senpi at around 4:44 pm UTC. That was just $1.02, or 0.91%, below the stock’s opening price.

The massive debut once again puts crypto’s growing pre-IPO futures market in the spotlight, especially as exchanges compete to attract traders who want to bet on major companies before their shares begin trading on Wall Street. SpaceX’s debut earlier this year also drew interest from crypto traders betting on its stock price through crypto futures.

Perpetual futures allow traders to bet on whether prices will rise or fall without owning the underlying shares, and unlike traditional futures they have no expiration date and can trade around the clock — including before a company goes public.

Trading on Hyperliquid was especially strong just before Anthropic shares opened, with the contract on trade.xyz recording $545.7 million in trading volume over 24 hours and $303.3 million in open interest (OI), according to Senpi data.

The contract was also trading at a 2.58% premium to its oracle price, the same data showed.

This interest had been growing for weeks, according to a Thursday report from crypto research firm Block Scholes, which found that daily trading volume for Anthropic futures on Hyperliquid averaged $75 million since Nov. 5, up from $7 million in September.

Traders also appeared to be betting on Anthropic’s stock rising, according to Token Terminal data, which showed that 5,567 traders held long positions in Anthropic futures on Hyperliquid on Wednesday, compared with 1,996 holding short positions.

Open interest in Anthropic perpetual futures on Hyperliquid reached a record $186 million on Nov. 18. Source: Token Terminal

OI across Anthropic futures markets on Hyperliquid also reached a record $186 million on Wednesday. Of that, $86 million came from trade.xyz contracts, while Entropy.io accounted for about $100 million.

Still, Anthropic futures made up just 1% of Hyperliquid’s total trading volume and accounted for only 3% of the increase in non-crypto trading since September, the Block Scholes report found.

Related: SpaceX tokenized IPO campaign draws $557M on Binance ahead of debut

Binance recorded about $741 million in Anthropic perpetual futures trading over 24 hours, compared with $195 million on OKX, according to DefiLlama. Entropy’s Anthropic market, which also runs on Hyperliquid, recorded about $80 million.

Those three exchanges recorded $214 million, $34 million and $110 million in OI, respectively. Across 15 Anthropic perpetual futures markets, OI came in at a combined $520.4 million as of 4:50 pm UTC.

Anthropic shares were trading at $98.49 as of 5:43 pm UTC on Thursday, down about 12% from their $111.50 opening price but still nearly 19% above the $83 IPO price.

Anthropic joins Saudi Aramco and SpaceX as one of just three companies to debut on public markets with a trillion-dollar valuation.

The Ethereum Foundation-linked wallet dumped another 2,500 $ETH ($3.88M), according to the Arkham database.

Wallet “0x5CBC” holds around $44.8 million in crypto, including $38.4 million of SUSDS and $6.41 million of Ethereum. Its latest transfer was on November 21, 2026.

Previously, another two wallets linked to the Ethereum Foundation transferred 4,393 ETH ($11.1 million at that time) to the Kraken exchange on October 12.

This transactions are not impulsive moves but part of a carefully planned 10-year strategy. Over the past decade, a total of 49,751 ETH has been sent from these addresses to exchanges.

The average selling price was a modest $658 by today's standards, although it’s worth noting that these coins were originally acquired in the project's early days, when ETH was worth less than a dollar.

Ethereum is currently going through one of its most challenging times in recent memory. Investor sentiment is still low, network activity has dispersed throughout Layer-2 solutions.

In addition to that, Ethereum has spent most of 2026 underperforming many rival ecosystems.

Earlier this year, Hsiao-Wei Wang has stepped down from the roles of co-executive director and board member of the Ethereum Foundation. In February, fellow co-Executive Director Tomasz Stańczak also stepped down.

Ethereum foundation has been selling ETH in 2026 during steep price movements, both up and down. For instance, BitMine acquired 10,000 ETH at an average price of $2,292 this June.

A similar sale one week earlier, where another 10,000 ETH was sold at $2,387 per coin.

On Friday, Bitcoin price dropped below $60,000. At some point, it fell 5.10% to $55,830, the lowest level since September 2024.

Bitcoin saw $554.28 million in liquidations, and 91.3% of that amount came from long positions.

Ethereum fell below $1,700 on Nov. 20, and ETH traders saw $331.08 million in liquidations.

#5120. VanEck ends Gemini deal for 3 crypto ETFs – What’s next for VSOL?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

VanEck has terminated Gemini as custodian and clearing agent for three U.S. crypto ETFs. In doing so, it has made Coinbase Custody its sole remaining safekeeper, thus putting into jeopardy the Solana fund’s staking future.

As per three 8-K filings from the 19th of November, VanEck Digital Assets delivered written termination notices to Gemini Trust Company on the 16th of November, with the contract set to officially end on 16th February.

The notice ended custody and clearing arrangements for the VanEck Bitcoin ETF (HODL) and the VanEck Ethereum ETF (ETHV).

The third ETF, the VanEck Solana ETF (VSOL), is the most affected one in this regard, because there is no news on who will take over staking services for it. Market participants are assuming it is Coinbase, but as of now, there is no confirmation yet.

As expected, the filings have opened a series of questions, as they have not established the allocation of assets between Gemini and Coinbase. This, in turn, has made it impossible to quantify precisely what Gemini is losing in dollar terms.

The filing also failed to explain why VanEck chose to terminate the agreements now, with roughly three months of notice before the effective date. Moreover, they did not even address the staking question that matters most for VSOL shareholders.

This comes as the crypto ETF landscape has been in a consolidation for a while now, with Coinbase already being a custodian for most U.S. Spot crypto ETFs.

All in all, VanEck’s current decision to put an end to its Gemini arrangements has left it with a single custodian across all three funds and a massive unresolved operational gap.

What’s left now is that by 16th February, 2027, the firm will have to find a new staking provider for VSOL.

#5119. Morning Minute: Google’s Newest AI Model Tops Benchmarks Ahead of Anthropic IPO

Morning Minute: Google’s Newest AI Model Tops Benchmarks Ahead of Anthropic IPO

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

Google shipped Gemini 4 Neon on Wednesday, its fourth frontier model release in three weeks, and on the eve of the heavily anticipated Anthropic IPO.

This isn’t just a normal model release. For one, it’s incredibly good. Neon posts 61 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index against 59 for Claude Opus 5.5 and 57 for GPT-6.2 Sol. It takes the Vals Index at 70.9%, AutomationBench at 56.3% against Opus 5.5’s 42.5%, and GraphWalks long-context retrieval at 95.2% against Opus 5.5’s 66.8%.

Beyond just the outperformance, Google has been releasing at a pace nobody else is matching. It dropped Argon in mid-October. Carbon on October 28. Hydrogen last week. Google owns its silicon, its data centers, and its distribution, and it is using all three to iterate faster than labs that rent compute. And it has some speculating that Google has already achieved RSI (recursive self-improvement), the final step ahead of super intelligence.

So Google has caught up at the top of the AI race. Just in time for the ANT IPO.

Anthropic will be priced at $83, 6.4% above the top of its $70 to $78 range, valuing the company around $1.5 trillion fully diluted. On the 825 million shares in the original terms, that’s roughly $68.5 billion raised, and about $94.5 billion counting the separate $26 billion private placement from its compute suppliers. It trades Thursday on Nasdaq as ANT. Notably, ANT has been trading on Hyperliquid in the $90-$100 range for the past week but popped to $105 this morning.

The valuation math is a 55% step-up from the $965 billion May round and about 25% below the $2 trillion Anthropic was reportedly chasing in September. Revenue ran $33.2 billion through nine months against $2.5 billion a year earlier, a 13x increase. Annualize that and $1.5 trillion is roughly 34 times sales.

That’s a steep raise given the macro backdrop and the Fed calling for more hikes for longer. AI stocks have been shaky over the past month and even moreso since Jensen Huang left Nvidia. Not the most confident market headed into the 2nd $1T+ IPO of the year.

So what does it mean for today’s price action? Personally, I expect a pop to the $95-$105 level that Hyperliquid has been pricing out for weeks, and it likely sees a spike higher. But there will be massive volatility, and it’s very likely to trade under that $83 level over the coming days and weeks. Especially given the new (surprise) headwinds from Google with its outperforming model.

We will find out soon enough. Good luck to everyone trading it today.

The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. The daily chart of XRP has yielded a death cross, the first time the signal has popped up in 2026. XRP experienced a death cross on its daily chart in November 2025 and then entered a downtrend for several months before a recovery in August 2026.

Buoyed by optimism around favorable crypto regulation, XRP had already seen a golden cross in September 2026 before this. But that didn’t last long. The Senate stalled on the Clarity Act, a bill on crypto market structure.

Since then, the wider crypto market has struggled. The declines deepened in late October, as concerns over artificial intelligence valuations and spending fueled a broad tech sell-off. They were compounded by the $6.3 billion Binance hack and weakening institutional ETF demand.

U.S. spot XRP ETFs recorded about $4.97 million in net outflows Friday, according to SoSoValue data, with the withdrawals concentrated in Bitwise's XRP fund.

Throughout November, the crypto market has struggled due to a combination of dollar strength and leveraged liquidations.

The 50 DMA fell below the 200 DMA on the daily chart, completing a death cross pattern, coinciding with a sharp decline that took XRP below a key support and left leveraged traders nursing heavy losses.

As seen on the monthly chart, XRP has declined below the monthly 50 MA, a key level that supported its 500% rise in November 2024.

However, all hope is not lost as the token’s daily relative strength index has dropped below 30, a level generally considered oversold. That can accompany a short-term rebound, but it does not fully establish that the decline has ended.

In a recent discussion on CNBC, Ripple CEO Brad Garlinghouse expressed hope for a crypto rebound.

Garlinghouse stated that the recent crypto sell-off reflects investors shifting funds into high-profile IPOs such as Anthropic. He expects to see the crypto markets bounce back after the listing, pointing to a similar rally after SpaceX’s IPO. If this scenario plays out, XRP will look to return to $1 and then $1.2. In contrast, support remains at $0.77 if the ongoing sell-off continues.

#5117. Bitcoin’s price hits ‘bottom territory’ after BTC falls below $60K — What next?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Less than half of all Bitcoin [BTC] is green right now, and that’s not a good look. In fact, as it turns out, we may not see light anytime soon.

As of 18th November, those are the numbers. At least according to the Rand Group.

Every major BTC bottom since 2015 has happened with less than half the supply in the green. In August 2025, that figure was 35.7%. It hit 39.8% in December 2018, 42.5% in March 2020, and 44.8% in November 2022.

Each low has come in a little higher than the one before. Holders are giving up less ground with every cycle. The argument is that the best opportunities show up when most holders are underwater, not when everyone’s celebrating.

Hence, the question: Does that mean the bottom is in? No, But Bitcoin is in a zone where that has mattered before.

… might be tech stocks and rates, according to QCP Capital.

Not everyone’s ready to call it a bottom, yet. In fact, the digital asset partner thinks Bitcoin’s problem is about money getting tight everywhere.

Earlier this cycle, ETF buying, corporate treasuries, and hopes of rate cuts did a good job of soaking up selling. That support will crack when stocks drop across the board.

So, what would it take to turn things around?

…recovery will likely require stabilisation in technology equities, reduced volatility in rates and evidence that institutional flows are returning.

Until then, any bounce driven by shorts closing out could fizzle fast. The Fed could also be another weight on BTC’s back.

At the time of writing, the charts weren’t really comforting either. BTC was trading at $58,681, down 5.2% over the last 24 hours. Just a couple of weeks ago, it was trading above $80K.

The RSI was knee deep in oversold territory, its lowest reading in months. Sellers have been running out fuel, but oversold conditions can stay as such for a while.

The ADX was 43.8 and still rising. So, the downtrend seemed to be getting stronger. Especially since buyers didn’t look like they were going to turn up.

This is a bottom range that we know. However, until the selling pressure slows down, calling it the floor would be a gamble.

#5116. Dozens of Seats Go Unclaimed At Donald Trump’s Memecoin Dinner

Home » Altcoins » Dozens of Seats Go Unclaimed At Donald Trump’s Memecoin Dinner

Presently, only 130 crypto whale wallets qualified for Trump’s fancy memecoin dinner at the golf club.

Just 130 crypto wallets secured seats for the November 22 TRUMP token holder dinner at Donald Trump’s Virginia golf club, leaving dozens of the 185 available places unclaimed. The Official Trump token was trading near record lows at the time of the close.

The event is designed to reward the largest holders, with the top 29 wallets also eligible for a VIP reception. Contest participants held roughly $8.6 million in tokens combined when the qualification period ended, a sharp drop in engagement from the project’s first holder dinner.

BREAKING: 🇺🇸 Trump will host an exclusive dinner for the top 185 $TRUMP holders this Sunday.The coin is down 99% from its peak. pic.twitter.com/NxizWHlrVg

Ranking was based on time-weighted holdings, which encouraged accumulation and retention through the window.

More than 5,000 eligible holders reportedly did not register. The leaderboard’s combined value represented only a small fraction of TRUMP’s roughly $268 million market capitalization.

TRUMP was trading around $0.95 in the latest reporting, down roughly 46% over the prior month after setting a record low near $0.896 on November 14. The token remains well below its peak, with one account describing it as down almost 99% from its high.

Large holders added to positions during the dip. Nansen data showed the top 100 wallets increased their combined holdings by 1.27% over 30 days, while a separate whale cohort expanded holdings by 26.63%. Exchange balances fell slightly over the same period.

Top-ranked winners risk losing VIP benefits if balances drop below specified levels before the event, and lower-ranked winners can forfeit their seat by selling more than half their qualifying balance. Those restrictions expire once the dinner is over.

Ahead of the 2025 dinner, 92 of 220 invited holders reportedly sold their entire positions before the event.

A Super Bowl-themed leaderboard planned for February 2027 may give some buyers a reason to stay involved, but it does not remove the immediate question of whether winners will take liquidity afterward.

#5115. MoonPay seeks US bank charter for stablecoin, custody services

MoonPay’s proposed national trust bank would offer stablecoin issuance and redemption, reserve management and digital asset custody under federal oversight.

Crypto payments firm MoonPay has applied for a US national trust bank charter to offer stablecoin issuance and redemption, reserve management and digital asset custody services.

According to an OCC filing, MoonPay submitted its application on Tuesday to establish MoonPay National Trust Bank, a non-depository institution headquartered in Miami Beach, Florida. The proposed bank would not accept traditional deposits or issue loans.

MoonPay already provides stablecoin issuance infrastructure to businesses, including support for branded digital dollars. If approved, the charter would allow its proposed banking subsidiary to provide stablecoin and custody services under federal supervision.

The application comes as US regulators prepare to implement the GENIUS Act, which takes effect on Jan. 18, 2027, and establishes federal standards for stablecoin issuers, including reserve backing and redemption requirements.

With the filing, MoonPay joins several crypto and payments companies seeking federal trust bank charters, including Rain, Modern Treasury and Block. Coinbase’s charter took effect earlier this month, while Circle received final OCC approval in July.

The rise in crypto firms applying for federal trust bank charters has drawn opposition from community banks. In October, the Independent Community Bankers of America sued the OCC, arguing that the regulator exceeded its authority by allowing non-depository trust banks to conduct activities traditionally associated with commercial banks.

#5113. Why TRUMP memecoin faces THESE risks ahead of Donald Trump’s exclusive dinner

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

What exactly could keep investors from maintaining their conviction in TRUMP?

For context, U.S. President Donald Trump is set to host an exclusive dinner for top holders of his TRUMP memecoin on Sunday. The timing is interesting. Politically, the Democrats’ victory in the midterm elections adds another layer of uncertainty for TRUMP holders. It could also increase scrutiny of Trump’s crypto-related ventures, making political developments an important factor to watch.

Another issue to note is that 10 wallets make up 80.6% of the overall token supply. So, while there are over 691k holders of TRUMP, a large percentage of these tokens are concentrated within a few wallets.

Against this backdrop, it’s no surprise that President Trump is hosting this dinner.

The logic is simple: If any large holders (there are 10 holders holding >80% of TRUMP) begin to sell TRUMP, it may cause panic selling. This risk is increased as TRUMP is already facing macroeconomic uncertainty.

So, is the dinner a “strategic” attempt to bring back interest in the memecoin?

The speculative capital in this cycle isn’t flowing into the memecoin market.

This marks a key difference from previous cycles, when investors often turned to memecoins during volatile market conditions to chase quick gains. But this time, the trend looks different. Despite broader market weakness, the memecoin market cap has continued to decline, with over $20 billion wiped out in Q4 so far.

According to AMBCrypto, this could bring some speculative capital back into memecoins.

Furthermore, as 80% of Official Trump [TRUMP] supply is concentrated in only 10 wallets, traders may be cautious of building up spot exposure.

Alternatively, they could favor short-term speculative trades to capitalize on  any event-driven price action. This could lead to increased speculation. However, due to weak market conditions, it may cause a new wave of de-leveraging and put more pressure on the larger crypto market.

TRUMP’s supply concentration and political uncertainty could weaken investor confidence, increasing the risk of sudden sell-offs.

Trump’s dinner may revive short-term memecoin speculation, but weak market conditions could trigger fresh deleveraging.

#5112. Quantum Upgrade Could Be 'Particularly Challenging' for Blockchains: IOSCO

Quantum Upgrade Could Be 'Particularly Challenging' for Blockchains: IOSCO

Financial markets, including crypto, need to start preparing now for quantum computers capable of breaking the cryptography that secures them, according to the world's main forum for securities regulators.

A "sufficiently capable" quantum computer could undermine widely used public-key cryptography that supports critical functions across financial markets, the International Organization of Securities Commissions (IOSCO) said in a report published on Thursday. The cyber readiness challenge posed by quantum computing "is immediate and requires action," it said.

"Practical quantum computers capable of compromising widely used cryptography do not yet exist, but the scale and duration of the transition mean the financial sector should start preparing today," IOSCO Board Chair Jean-Paul Servais said in a statement.

The 63-page report cited conservative estimates that such machines could emerge between 2030 and 2035. Several industry participants consulted by IOSCO went further, arguing that firms should prepare for the possibility that they arrive within five years, potentially accelerated by AI.

IOSCO said the migration may be particularly challenging for blockchains, because cryptography is embedded in their core architecture and any change requires coordinated action across decentralized networks. Public blockchains such as Ethereum, Solana and Tron rely heavily on elliptic curve cryptography to verify identities and validate transactions, and a powerful enough quantum computer could derive private keys from exposed public keys, a scenario known as "Q-Day."

Swapping in quantum-resistant signatures comes at a cost. Signatures under ML-DSA-44, a post-quantum standard from the U.S. National Institute of Standards and Technology, are about 2,420 bytes, roughly 35 to 40 times larger than today's 64-byte elliptic curve signatures, the report said. It cited evidence from a quantum-resistant Solana testnet that saw throughput fall by about 90% when post-quantum signatures replaced existing ones.

Because the cryptography sits in transaction formats and consensus rules, replacing it requires hard forks, and decentralized networks have no way to force quick adoption, making migration "slow and fragmented," IOSCO said. Accounts and smart contracts still controlled by vulnerable keys could stay exposed after a network adds quantum-resistant options, and the path to quantum resistance for Bitcoin wallets remains uphill.

Wallet makers face their own trade-offs. SHRINCS, the leading candidate for a post-quantum Bitcoin signature scheme, offers "stateful" signatures that are about 10 times smaller but can expose funds to theft if keys are reused beyond a set signing budget, Trezor CTO Tomáš Sušánka has said. Trezor is considering making the larger, safer stateless option the default, and hardware wallets will also need post-quantum cryptography for firmware and device authentication, he added.

The report distinguished "on-spend" attacks, in which an attacker races to exploit a public key revealed by a pending transaction, from "at-rest" attacks on keys left exposed for longer. Dormant or abandoned coins pose a particular problem where their holders can't move them. Crypto firms have formed a quantum alliance as some 6 million BTC sit in exposed addresses, and Bitcoin developer Cory Fields has proposed "quantum landmines" to make attacks on such coins costly.

Layer-2 networks are not insulated either. Optimistic rollups could face forged signatures during their dispute windows, typically seven days on Ethereum, and many zero-knowledge rollups use SNARK proofs that rely on elliptic curves. Hash-based STARK proofs are more resistant, but a rollup is only as safe as the layer-1 contracts, bridges and governance keys it depends on, the report said.

Developers are already working on fixes. Researchers have proposed several ways to make Bitcoin quantum-safe, StarkWare has shown a quantum-safe Bitcoin transaction, and Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin has said the network is betting its future on quantum resistance.

IOSCO said firms should migrate to post-quantum cryptography and build "crypto-agility," the ability to update cryptographic systems without major redesign. Quantum key distribution could complement those measures on selected high-security channels, it added.

Most preparations across financial markets are still at an early stage, focused on awareness, governance, training and identifying vulnerable systems, according to the report, which drew on a March 2026 survey of members of IOSCO's Fintech Task Force and discussions with experts. Some respondents described "quantum-procrastination," in which firms put off action while waiting for clearer regulatory signals or wider adoption.

No surveyed jurisdiction reported operational deployment of quantum computing in regulated financial services. The watchdog also urged regulators to act against "quantum washing," in which firms overstate the role, maturity or performance of quantum technology. IOSCO said it will treat quantum as a priority in 2027, focusing on raising awareness across the financial ecosystem.

Quantum hardware is advancing quickly. IBM has claimed a trusted quantum advantage, and Google has said it has cracked some of the field's biggest bottlenecks.

The massive spike in trading volume comes in the wake of one of the biggest sell-offs in BTC this year.

On Nov. 21, the world’s largest cryptocurrency dropped as low as around $55,100, a level last seen in September 2024. As a result, there has been a decline in the amount of stETH immediately available on exchanges for potential selling.

This comes as Ethereum was trading near $1,753.91 at press time, after a drop of 2.9% in the past 24 hours.

At the same time, around 836,798 ETH is currently waiting to enter Ethereum’s validator network. Of this, the estimated waiting period currently stands at 14 days and 13 hours, with a processing rate (churn) of 256 ETH per epoch.

Interestingly, all of this coincided with a recent report by AMBCrypto where Ethereum’s staking ratio has reportedly reached an all-time high of 36.3%, with approximately 44.5 million ETH locked in staking, up from 42.1 million in mid-August.

Meanwhile, institutional players such as BitMine and SharpLink continue expanding their staking activities, with BitMine holding 5.22 million staked ETH and earning $121.4 million from staking and validation.

#5109. Anthony Scaramucci Predicts Bitcoin Bottom This Week

Bitcoin (BTC) bull and SkyBridge Capital founder Anthony Scaramucci has argued that the world's biggest cryptocurrency could find a critical bottom as soon as this week.

Scaramucci's comments follow the leading cryptocurrency's struggle to recover from a catastrophic crypto market crash that took BTC to its lowest price level in over two years.

In the latest episode of All Things Markets on Nov. 20, the SkyBridge Capital founder joined Galaxy Digital CEO Mike Novogratz to discuss a potential bottom for Bitcoin.

Scaramucci shared his view that Bitcoin could see its bottom on Nov. 25, citing the cryptocurrency's four-year halving cycle as the driving force behind his comments.

"Before we came on, I asked ChatGPT when's the expected halving for Bitcoin," Scaramucci explained.

"And then when are we 500 days out from that halving? And the estimate was November 25th," he added.

This prediction stems from the idea that Bitcoin's price trends could follow a predictable four-year cycle that coincides with the cryptocurrency's halving events, when the new BTC supply is cut in half.

This theory suggests that Bitcoin could find its major bottom around the 500-day mark before its next halving, expected in 2028.

However, these theories are by no means a certainty when it comes to predicting future price action, especially with the current level of institutional participation and macroeconomic forces impacting the crypto market to an unprecedented extent.

During the conversation, Novogratz also took note of Bitcoin's history of forming double bottoms during its four-year cycles.

"And every four years, Bitcoin double bottomed," Novogratz remarked.

At the time, the Galaxy Digital CEO's comments suggested a potentially positive scenario, as Bitcoin had been able to hold its psychological $60,000 price level.

"If it doesn't break 60, that could be a good sign," he noted.

Unfortunately for Bitcoin, the cryptocurrency subsequently crashed below this critical price point. This has left some wondering whether the cryptocurrency has already found its ultimate bottom.

During the same conversation, Novogratz suggested that Bitcoin's short-term outlook could largely depend on the current situation in the stock market.

Specifically, the billionaire said that a rebound in tech stocks could lead to a strong rally in Bitcoin's price.

"If we put in a bottom in the next couple days in the S&P here, down 3% from here, down 5% from here. And we have a rally back up. Man, Bitcoin is going to rally hard with it," Novogratz predicted.

However, he also warned that Bitcoin could see a lot of market-driven movements going forward, as opposed to crypto-specific movements.

"I think we're going to be stuck tethered to general sentiment and risk," he said.

Novogratz also believes that Bitcoin could trade in a range between $60,000 and $75,000 before a breakout or breakdown becomes apparent.

Bitcoin plunged to around $55,100 on Nov. 21, its lowest price level since September 2024, following another severe wave of sell-offs in the crypto market.

It then bounced back strongly to climb above $60,000 again, albeit briefly, before falling back to around $59,000 on Nov. 22.

Binance obtains court orders freezing assets stolen in October’s hack. Hong Kong warns citizens about Hyperliquid, and Thai police arrest four foreigners linked to a crypto scam.

Binance has secured a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing injunction from the Abu Dhabi Global Market Courts as it seeks to recover assets stolen in its record Oct. 29 hack.

The court orders target unidentified perpetrators of the attack and are intended to help issuers, exchanges and other partners freeze and return stolen assets,

The orders can be served via blockchain, allowing legal notices to be delivered to wallet addresses rather than through traditional channels. Market participants will also be notified of updates to a “living list” of 26,000 wallet addresses identified as holding stolen assets.

Despite losing billions in the hack, Binance’s APAC head SB Seker told Reuters this week its finances are sound and it plans to expand throughout South East Asia.

Hong Kong’s Securities and Futures Commission (SFC) added decentralized perps exchange Hyperliquid to its Alert List. The SFC said Hyperliquid lacks a license to operate in the city and is suspected of actively marketing its services to the region’s public.

The SFC is now officially suspicious of 69 platforms.

The Hong Kong Monetary Authority and Swift will explore linking the HKMA’s market infrastructure, Project Ensemble, with Swift’s ledger, for cross-border payments using tokenized deposits in multiple currencies.

The Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS) issued 100 million Singapore dollars ($77.5 million) in tokenized bills on Nov. 6, with banks using wholesale central bank digital currency (CBDC) to pay for them. Five banks submitted bids totaling more than twice the amount on offer, MAS said Thursday. The four-week bills mature on Dec. 4.

Singapore’s DBS has partnered with Circle to broaden customer access to USDC across Asia.

CryptoJobsList has acquired its rival crypto jobs service Crypto.jobs.

Wisdom Tree has registered three tokenized funds in Singapore and is working with Synthesys to make them available through regulated intermediaries.

Citadel Securities is expanding its Singapore and Sydney offices by 40% and 65% respectively.

The Shanghai Municipal Science and Technology Commission and the Shanghai Municipal Bureau of Data have issued a new policy that encourages the adoption of blockchain and other technologies as a way to share and track scientific data.Meanwhile the Cyberspace Administration of China has clarified the names of blockchain information services are not allowed to include words like “virtual currency”, “exchange” and “ICO”

Agribusiness solutions company Bunge and feed ingredient trading firm Bangkok Produce Merchandising Company have expanded their blockchain based soy traceability initiative to Vietnam. The collaboration has already established traceable soybean and soybean meal supply chains from Brazil to Thailand.

Thai police have arrested four foreign nationals in Pattaya over an alleged scam that defrauded victims of more than 400 million baht ($11.8M) through fake crypto investments.The operation followed the rescue of a 19-year-old Chinese student held for ransom three days earlier and uncovered a crypto romance scam allegedly run by three Chinese nationals and one Taiwanese fugitive.

#5106. Gen Z Embraces Crypto While Most Americans Call It a 'Side Quest'

Gen Z Embraces Crypto While Most Americans Call It a 'Side Quest'

For many young Americans, crypto is coming before the 401(k).

Some 21% of Gen Z adults own cryptocurrency, compared with 16% who hold an employer-sponsored retirement plan like a 401(k), according to a survey released Thursday by SoFi Technologies.

That’s seven times the rate of baby boomers, just 3% of whom own crypto. Crypto was Gen Z’s most widely held investment in the survey, ahead of stocks, bonds ,and real estate.

The online survey of 2,064 U.S. adults was conducted by SoFi and YouGov from Oct. 15 to 28. SoFi, a nationally chartered bank that sells crypto alongside checking and investment accounts, developed the report with Culture Co-op.

Gen Z respondents described crypto less as a gamble than a strategy. They were nearly three times as likely as the general population to call it one of the best ways to build future wealth, 21% vs. 8%, and 61% said they see it as a financial advantage unique to their generation.

Only 20% of Gen Z crypto owners said they bought in because it “sounded interesting,” compared with 34% of crypto investors overall. Diversification (32%) and easier international payments (29%) ranked higher.

Part of the gap reflects age. Many respondents in their late teens and 20s don’t yet have access to an employer retirement plan.

Across the broader population, the picture is far cooler. Only 13% of Americans own crypto, and 62% are firm skeptics. Three in four say crypto feels intimidating to get into, and 77% agreed with the statement, “Cryptocurrency feels like a financial side quest that I don’t have the time or energy for.”

Even owners feel out of their depth: 82% of current crypto investors said they should know more about it than they do, and 11% admitted knowing little to nothing.

“Nobody feels the gaps in their financial education more sharply than someone who has just bought Bitcoin,” said Natalie Brunell, host of the Coin Stories podcast and a contributor to SoFi’s crypto newsletter.

The findings echo a recent survey conducted by Ipsos for the DeFi Education Fund, which found that 22% of Americans have ever owned or used crypto but only 13% used it in the past year.

SoFi argues that putting crypto in the same app as banking is the key to wider adoption. Half of respondents open to crypto said having their primary bank offer it would be a tipping point, and 63% of current investors said they’d trade more if they could do it at their bank.

SoFi has been building out that model, launching a tokenized deposit that pays 4.4% and moves like a stablecoin, as well as a bank-issued stablecoin running on Polygon’s payments infrastructure.

The survey also challenges the crypto “tech bro” stereotype. Black and Hispanic respondents own crypto at more than twice the rate of white respondents, 22% to 9%. Miami led the 10 cities surveyed at 20% ownership, while San Francisco matched the national average at 13%.

#5105. Ripple ETF Investors Hold On, But XRP Buyers Go Missing

Home » Trendsetter News » Ripple ETF Investors Hold On, But XRP Buyers Go Missing

XRP ETF assets fell 31.6% despite modest redemptions, suggesting a lack of fresh demand, not a buyer exodus.

Ripple’s XRP-linked ETF assets fell 31.6% from September 30 through the Friday cited in the video, compared with a 20.2% decline for Bitcoin funds. Yet redemptions remained modest.

Dana Love, PhD, a popular market analyst examining ETF flows and corporate filings, argues that missing buyers, not an investor stampede, help explain the XRP’s deeper slide this month.

The XRP coin traded at $0.967 this Friday morning, following a last Friday low of $0.89.

Dana Love’s central claim: Bitcoin-related selling is weighing on XRP, while demand from XRP funds and a closely watched treasury company has failed to provide much support.

According to the figures presented, cumulative net inflows into XRP ETFs slipped from $1.809 billion on October 29 to $1.789 billion on Monday. That leaves roughly 98.9% of cumulative net inflows intact, despite the sharp decline in fund assets.

But November through that Monday brought no positive daily net flows. “Nobody is running for the exits,” the analyst says, “but nobody new was walking in.”

Bitcoin ETFs, meanwhile, recorded roughly $2.34 billion in November outflows through the same period. Monday’s $8 million inflow interrupted the selling, although the analyst cautions that one positive session does not establish a reversal.

The distinction matters: limited XRP ETF redemptions do not establish that XRP holders across the entire market are refusing to sell. Nor does a decline in fund assets measure withdrawals alone; falling token prices also reduce those balances.

Dana Love also questions Evernorth, the XRP treasury company whose October 14 release said it would “accumulate and deploy” XRP into institutional strategies beginning in the fourth quarter of 2026.

The video estimates that Evernorth had about $111 million in cash, including subsequent warrant proceeds, and says its shares still traded above estimated underlying asset value.

That could support further fundraising, although the company had not announced additional purchases in the period discussed.

Silence is not proof of inactivity. The analyst explicitly acknowledges that Evernorth may be buying without announcing every transaction.

For investors, the useful distinction is between broader market relief and renewed XRP-specific market demand.

Sustained Bitcoin ETF inflows would suggest selling pressure is easing; XRP inflows arriving independently, or a disclosed Evernorth purchase, would offer stronger evidence that XRP has found buyers of its own.

#5104. XRP Ledger Loses 60% of Users

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

XRP Ledger's network activity has dropped sharply, losing around 60% of the users it had yesterday. The decline comes amid a serious price correction and increased pressure on the cryptocurrency market. Selling pressure remains high, and the declining number of users is not helping the price.

XRPScan's data showed 250,000 users 24 hours ago, while today's reading shows barely 92,000 users. While the drop is significant, it should not be considered critical on its own. The decline partly reflects a regular weekend slowdown, when traders are generally less active, but it comes alongside the price correction and broader selling pressure.

The highest point for this metric in November was 300,000, with a monthly average of approximately 200,000 active users. The number of users is a critical metric for the network's health, and a decline toward 100,000 often comes before bullish sentiment among investors. The current drop does not guarantee that sentiment will turn, especially as the price remains under pressure.

Apart from the negative on-chain trend, XRP's price performance has also been weak. The price lost roughly 5% from its November 19 close and even fell below the $0.90 price level, which was expected to serve as a foundation for recovery. XRP was around $0.915 on November 22 after closing at $0.9604 three days earlier.

The trend remains bearish, and the decline could become significantly worse.

Few expected a surge in market liquidations so soon, as Bitcoin fell below the $60,000 price level. XRP and other altcoins followed the broader market trend and suffered serious losses.

Interest in the network appears to be weakening as the price correction continues, so this drop in activity is not helping XRP's recovery.

A potential recovery above $0.9 could be the key to a quick rebound. However, the overall trend remains bearish and is unlikely to change unless significant market events occur.

#5103. Coinbase could claim a betting floor of their own, but what about Kalshi?

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Coinbase wants a piece of the prediction markets action, with the exchange reportedly planning a launch next year.

Right now, they don’t run their own; they borrow them. Users trade event contracts on the exchange through a Kalshi partnership. That could change next year though.

Citing sources close to the matter, a Bloomberg report claimed that Coinbase wants to roll out its own event contracts in the U.S in H1 2027. It would be open to both retail and institutional traders.

But why bother? For starters, Coinbase gets to decide what launches and when. Next, it keeps a bigger share of the trading fees.

There’s real money at stake here. In Q3 2026, Coinbase’s prediction markets business was already on track to bring in over $200 million a year. If you think about it, this isn’t an overnight decision. In the run up to it all, Coinbase announced that it was buying prediction market startup The Clearing Company in December 2025.

Then, in September this year, the CFTC gave the green light for its own clearing house.

All that being said, nothing’s set in stone. However, the pieces are clearly falling into place.

Crypto trading may have slowed down, but prediction markets have kept themselves out of the bleeding.

Since launch, revenue from the business has grown by 82% QoQ on average. In Q3 alone, daily traders went up by over 60%, compared to the quarter before.

CFO Alesia Haas summed up the appeal pretty neatly. She said,

Prediction markets are adding another revenue stream and are less correlated to crypto volatility…

A big deal for an exchange that lives and dies by market cycles.

On the earnings call, Haas also said that the new clearing house would let Coinbase run its own contracts and earn more from them.

Worth pointing out, however, that Kalshi isn’t being cut out as partner contracts will stay on the platform.

"Shib.io is home to both Shibarium and ShibaSwap," Shiba Inu developer and long-time community member Mazrael said, citing the project’s public Git repository as evidence of the longstanding relationship.

Mazrael recently spoke about the link between the official web presence of the Shiba Inu ecosystem and its decentralized platforms in a post, stating that the information has been consistent since the launch of ShibaSwap.

@Heisenbergiqr3 @tacitfi @randhindi @LCSHIB @Shibtoken https://t.co/RP8CH8r1zW is home of Shibarium and Shibaswap as indicated in our public git that has not changed since Shibaswap and is the same git for shibarium

He pointed to the project’s public Git as proof that Shib.io is the home of Shibarium and ShibaSwap.

The clarification strengthens the link among Shib.io, ShibaSwap and Shibarium, two key components of the Shiba Inu ecosystem.

Stablecoins are crypto’s ready-to-deploy cash, so large whale balances can represent potential buying power for BTC and altcoins. Falling balances can mean funds were deployed, redeemed, transferred elsewhere, or moved outside these tracked wallets. Either way, having less USDT and USDC concentrated among major holders means less obvious dry powder waiting on the sidelines.

#5101. Best Hyperliquid trading bots: AI agents and bots compared

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Hyperliquid has become one of the busiest venues for crypto perpetual futures, and a long list of tools now automate trading on it. They differ less in what they promise than in two practical questions: who decides the trades, and what the tool is allowed to do with your account. This guide compares the main Hyperliquid trading bots and AI trading agents in 2026, plus Hyperliquid’s vaults, against those questions. Each tool was compared on who decides the trades, what its key can do with your account, and what evidence of performance exists, using each provider’s own documentation in October 2026.

Most Hyperliquid tools connect through an API wallet, which Hyperliquid also calls an agent wallet. The account owner approves a separate key that can sign orders for the account but cannot withdraw or transfer funds. These approvals expire, and approving a new wallet under the same name replaces the old one. Some tools also recommend a sub-account, which keeps the bot’s balances and positions separate. The assets stay in the user’s own Hyperliquid account throughout, which is why most Hyperliquid bots can fairly be called non-custodial.

Many tools charge through a builder fee, a small fee added to each filled order. It applies only after the user approves a maximum fee from the main wallet, and Hyperliquid caps it at 0.1% on perpetuals.

Tools are grouped by who supplies the trading logic: a managed model first, then signal execution, rule-based bots and open-source software (3Commas sits with the rule-based bots). Vaults come last because they replace a bot rather than run one. Hyperliquid’s own trading and funding fees apply to every option.

AlphaNet is built for Hyperliquid and supplies the trading logic itself. Users pick one of its deep learning strategies for crypto perpetual futures and an allocation, and the model decides when to enter and exit each trade. It connects the way the exchange intends: the user approves an agent wallet that can place and cancel orders but cannot withdraw or transfer, and trades run from the user’s own account. New deployments allow leverage up to 3× account equity. On cost, AlphaNet’s Hyperliquid trading bot charges no platform fee during the current promotional period, compared with builder fees of 0.035–0.1% per fill on several bots below; Hyperliquid’s protocol fees still apply, and a dynamic trading fee applies afterwards.

A newer group of AI agent projects on Hyperliquid connect language models to an API wallet and let them place trades. They are mostly early. In the first season of Alpha Arena, a short real-money competition on Hyperliquid, four of the six language models that took part finished with losses and two with gains.

WunderTrading is built for traders whose strategy already exists as TradingView alerts or external signals. It turns those alerts into Hyperliquid orders and also offers DCA and grid bots, charging a 0.035% builder fee with no separate subscription.

goodcryptoX is a no-code app for grid, DCA, trailing, and TradingView bots on iOS, Android, and the web. Gainium covers similar bot types and adds backtesting and paper trading, with a choice between a free mode that adds a builder fee and paid plans that do not. Bitsgap brings its hosted grid and DCA bots to Hyperliquid alongside the centralised exchanges it already supports, and 3Commas, one of the most widely used bot platforms, lists Hyperliquid among its supported exchanges for spot and futures. With all of them, the bot executes your logic; the tool does not supply a trading edge.

Hummingbot has Hyperliquid spot and perpetual connectors and includes market-making scripts and arbitrage, TWAP, and grid executors. The self-run client charges no builder fee. It accepts either an API wallet or the main wallet’s private key; the second gives the software full control of the account, so the API wallet is the safer choice.

Rule-based bots have no platform-level record, because results depend on each user’s settings. Vaults are the most transparent option: every vault’s history is on-chain and visible in Hyperliquid’s app. Managed strategy platforms sit in between. AlphaNet publishes a running record of each strategy since its start date, and because trades run from users’ own accounts, each user’s fills are on-chain and can be checked against it.

Instead of connecting a tool to your own account, you deposit into a vault that a leader trades. The protocol’s own vault, HLP, provides liquidity, performs liquidations, and earns a share of fees. User-run vaults follow individual strategies. Leaders take 10% of profits and must keep at least 5% of the vault, and deposits are locked for one day (four days for HLP).

The choice comes down to the two questions this guide started with. First, decide who you want making the trades: your own rules, a signal you already trust, a trained model, or a vault leader. Then check what the tool is allowed to do with your account. On Hyperliquid, most tools need only an agent wallet that can trade but not withdraw, so there is rarely a reason to hand over a main wallet key or move funds into someone else’s control.

Two further checks are worth making before committing real capital. The first is cost per fill: builder fees on top of Hyperliquid’s own fees can add up. The second is the record: a vault’s history is on-chain, a managed strategy should publish a live one, and a rule-based bot has none until you build your own.

Big-time speed performance makes Solana edge the apex crypto asset, but both are in losing key thresholds.

Solana is showing relative strength against Bitcoin while traders monitor a key level that could support a short-term bottom, according to recent analysis from More Crypto Online and Crypto Tony.

More Crypto Online highlighted that Solana (SOL) is starting to outperform Bitcoin (BTC) on the 4-hour and daily Solana price charts.

#5099. Ethereum Drafts New Gas Rules for Validator Deposits

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Ethereum developers want to change the gas accounting for validator deposits. The plan is to measure the request bytes that these deposit events create.

The gas calculation already accounts for executing a deposit. However, after the deposit is finished, a 192-byte request is passed along with the block. Those 192 bytes are excluded from the minimum gas charge, so the same gas limit might allow more data than the initial calculation accounts for.

Under Gloas, there is no fixed cap on the number of deposit requests per block. The execution layer has not capped this either. Gas is the only limiting factor.

Neither EIP-8131 nor EIP-8279 accounts for those request bytes. This means that deposits could become another way to bypass the gas floor once EIP-8279 closes the loophole involving cold storage reads.

A relay contract can call the deposit contract repeatedly, costing around 26,000 gas per deposit. In the author's example, that execution cost is below the point where the gas floor for zero-byte calldata would start to increase the total charge.

This results in a payload of about 1.44 times the block gas limit divided by 64, compared with about 1.15 times the same baseline.

The main issue is the data size, so the proposal directly addresses the data.

A log from the deposit contract with the deposit event topic counts as 192 bytes before the log entry is added. This is the same condition EIP-6110 uses to turn logs into requests.

The cost of this would be another check that client developers would have to implement and test.

Users are not expected to pay more for regular deposits. The cost of executing a regular deposit is already above the proposed minimum, so the extra 12,288 gas only raises the minimum charge.

The proposal has yet to be scheduled for a network upgrade.
